# Fees101 — Roadmap & Build Checklist

> Living document. Single source of truth for what we've built, what's stubbed,
> and what's still needed. Check items off (`- [x]`) as they ship. Add, edit, or
> remove freely — this file is meant to be edited by hand.
>
> **Working rule:** whenever an enhancement, idea, or "we should do X later" comes
> up in any working session, it gets added here immediately so nothing is lost.
>
> Last reviewed against the codebase: **2026-07-31**

## 🧭 What Fees101 is

**Fees101 is not a School Management System. It is a School Revenue Operations Platform.**

The bet: remove cash-handling friction (autonomous bank-transfer collection via DVA, manual/cash entry off by default) and remove parent-portal friction (SMS/email instead of a login) — not build a general-purpose school ERP. Attendance, results, timetables, LMS, HR/payroll are explicitly out of scope unless a school forces the question. Everything in this roadmap should be read against that positioning — scope creep toward "manage the whole school" is a deliberate no.

### 🎯 Target: onboard the first real school by **end of November 2026**

Every session works toward that date. Rough intent:
- **Now → build features that don't depend on live Monnify or live messaging** (both are deferred but tracked below).
- **Auth & onboarding come LAST** — no 2FA / friction while testing/logging in daily.
- **Before launch:** fresh DB rebuild, UI rebuild, docs + help center, go-live hardening.

Legend: `[ ]` not started · `[~]` partial/in progress · `[x]` done

---

- [x] **URL migration to the new 7-workspace IA — shipped 2026-09-22, full route sweep verified.** The Modernist redesign had only relabeled/regrouped the existing routes into the sidebar workspaces (`navConfig.ts`) without ever renaming the actual URLs. Migrated project-wide: `/dashboard` → `/today` (with `/activity` nested as `/today/activity`), `/invoices` + `/payments` + `/reports` consolidated under `/money/{invoices,collections,reports}`, `/settings/*` split into `/school/*` (profile, academic structure, payments, reminders, discount policy) and `/team/*` (users, roles, audit log, data & privacy, account security). `/students/*`, `/fees/*`, `/discounts`, `/login` and all `/api/*` routes are unchanged. Every `redirect()` fallback, `revalidatePath()` call, nav `href`/`match` array, `middleware.ts` protected-path list, and hardcoded `<Link>`/`router.push` across ~45 files was updated to match (also incidentally closed a pre-existing middleware gap where `/payments`/`/reports` were never in the protected-paths list). Added `next.config.ts` redirects for every old path so existing bookmarks/links 307 to their new destination instead of 404ing. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Verified live: full route sweep for the owner (22 routes), all legacy bookmark redirects (16 old paths, including cascading two-hop redirects for permission-gated fallbacks), and a re-run of the Bursar/Accounts-clerk/Class-teacher permission sweep — all nav visibility, tab bars, and permission-fail redirects resolved identically to pre-migration behavior on the new URLs.
- [x] **Phone-verification resend cap — shipped 2026-09-22.** Caps SMS sends for school phone verification at 3 per number per rolling 24-hour window (`db/phone_verify_resend_limit.sql`, new `phone_verify_send_count`/`phone_verify_window_started_at` columns on `schools`), on top of the existing 60s inter-resend cooldown — so a number that can never be verified (e.g. no carrier coverage) can't burn unlimited SMS credit. Resets automatically when the window ages out, and resets immediately if the phone number itself changes. Surfaced via the existing error-notice UI in `SchoolProfileForm.tsx`, no new UI needed.
- [x] **Removed the root "Coming soon" placeholder — shipped 2026-09-22.** `src/app/page.tsx` (`/`) now redirects straight to `/login` instead of showing a static placeholder page, since marketing lives at fees101.com and this app's root only needs to be the entry point. Reuses `middleware.ts`'s existing auth-path handling — an already-logged-in visitor hitting `/` still lands on `/today`, since middleware bounces authenticated users off `/login` anyway. Verified live both logged-out (`/` → `/login`) and logged-in (`/` → `/today`).
- [x] **Naming/URL consistency fix — shipped 2026-09-22.** The Today workspace's activity tab was called "Record" (tab label + page heading) but "Stream" in the sidebar footer, and its URL was still `/today/activity` even after both labels agreed. Standardized on "Record" everywhere and renamed the route to `/today/record`, with `/today/activity` and the older `/activity` both redirecting to it.
- [x] **Money → Reports split into Export / History sub-tabs, with History paginated — shipped 2026-09-22.** The single-page "Reports and exports" list plus "Download history" table is now two client-state tabs (`ReportsLayout.tsx`, same local-tab pattern as Discounts' Queue/Recurring) so the history table doesn't compete for space and can grow without bound. `getReportDownloads` split into the original small unfiltered fetch (feeds each report card's "last run") and a new `getReportDownloadHistory` that does real server-side pagination + the report-type filter via `.range()` + `{ count: 'exact' }` (`src/lib/reports/reports.ts`), replacing the old client-side-only filter over a flat 25-row fetch. `money/reports/page.tsx` now reads `page`/`perPage`/`reportType` from `searchParams`, same URL-param convention as `/today/record`.
- [x] **Cycles page: removed the redundant "Generate N missing" footer link, clarified "Print all" — shipped 2026-09-22.** The active-term footer repeated the exact same "Generate N missing" action already shown (with more prominence — progress bar, state label) on step 01, so it added nothing and was removed. "Print all (N)" renamed to "Print all invoices (N)" everywhere it appears (`CyclesLayout.tsx` active/draft/closed footers, `CycleDetailLayout.tsx`) so it reads unambiguously as an invoices action, per user feedback that it wasn't obvious what it printed.
- [x] **Money → Collections "Trajectory across terms" chart looked stretched — shipped 2026-09-22.** `InkLineChart` (`PaymentsDashboard.tsx`) rendered its 420×180 viewBox with `preserveAspectRatio="none"` at `width: 100%` — with only 2-3 terms to compare (the common case), that stretched two short line segments across the entire dashboard column into an exaggerated, distorted-looking spike. The chart now sizes itself from the actual point count (~130px per point, capped 320-760px) instead of always filling the panel, so a short series renders at a natural width and only grows toward the cap as more terms/sessions are compared. Verified live with a 3-term compare — chart now sits at ~540px instead of spanning the full ~1900px content column.
- [x] **Every browser tab said the same "Fees101" — distinct per-route titles shipped 2026-09-22.** Root cause of a reported "the URL looks the same on two different pages" confusion: `src/app/layout.tsx` had one static, global `<title>` with zero per-page variation, so no two tabs/browser-history entries were ever visually distinguishable. Root layout now sets `title: { default: 'Fees101', template: '%s · Fees101' }`, and every one of the ~24 real routes (all workspace pages, the three detail pages under `students/[id]`, `money/invoices/[id]`, `fees/cycles/[id]`, and the three client-rendered auth pages via a `document.title` effect since `export const metadata` isn't available in a Client Component) now sets its own `title`. In-page tabs that are client state rather than routes (Discounts' Queue/Recurring, Reports' Export/History) still share one title per the underlying route — unchanged, since they were never separate URLs either. Verified live across several routes. Deferred: the three `[id]` detail pages use a static label ("Student", "Invoice", "Term overview") rather than the specific record's name — doable later via `generateMetadata` with a light dedicated query, held back for now since the existing page-body queries are already flagged as heavy and doing it via `generateMetadata` would mean a second DB round-trip purely for the title.
- [x] **Student detail page now shows a credit balance — shipped 2026-09-22.** A student's running `credit_balance` (overpayment/opt-out refund-in-kind, auto-applied against their next generated invoice by `computeInvoice.ts`) was already being fetched by `getStudentPaymentHistory` into `summary.unappliedCredit`, but nothing on the page ever rendered it — a school had no way to see a student was carrying credit until it silently reduced a future invoice. Added a "CREDIT BALANCE" figure to the identity header (`students/[id]/page.tsx`), in the same grid as "OUTSTANDING NOW", shown only when the balance is greater than zero (kept out of the header for the common case of no credit). No new query — the field was already fetched and unused.
- [x] **Unsent-invoice-change backstop and gross+credit display re-verified against the Modernist redesign — shipped 2026-09-22.** Prompted by the owner asking whether invoice regeneration still notifies when a resend is needed and whether "resend" still exists after the redesign rewrite. Investigation found the backstop from 2026-09-14 (dashboard "Needs you" queue row, `previewCloseTerm`'s `unnotifiedChangedCount`, the close-term informational panel + "Resend now" button, and the unconditional audit-log count) had all survived the redesign intact — it now lives on `/today` (`today/page.tsx`) and the dedicated `/fees/close-term` page (`CloseTermLedger.tsx`) rather than the old dashboard/`CyclesLayout.tsx` modal the pre-redesign write-up named, but the logic and behaviour are unchanged. The gross+credit display did regress: the redesign's rewrite of `InvoicesListLayout.tsx` and `InvoiceDetailLayout.tsx` dropped it. Re-added: `InvoicesListLayout.tsx` now shows a "− ₦X credit" note (green) under the OUTSTANDING figure on any row with `creditApplied > 0` — the redesigned row has no separate Total column, so this sits under the row's most prominent money figure instead of literally "under Total"; `InvoiceDetailLayout.tsx`'s Subtotal row (which sits above the itemized "Credit balance applied" line) now also appears when credit alone is the adjustment, not just discount/previous-balance, completing the gross → credit → net chain the detail page already itemized. Verified live end-to-end: dashboard queue row and close-term panel checked with a real in-app-triggered `needs_resend` invoice (opt-in toggle → additive engine), confirmed visible to a *different* admin (Bursar test persona) who didn't cause the change and absent for a Class Teacher persona without `manage-invoices`; the "Resend now" panel opened with the correct count and copy, cancelled without sending (this dev environment has `SENDCHAMP_MODE=live`/`EMAIL_MODE=live`, so the final send confirmation was deliberately not clicked to avoid dispatching a real message to a real seeded contact); both list and detail credit displays confirmed with screenshots against a real invoice with an applied credit. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **Confirmed still deferred, unchanged from 2026-09-14:** `needsRegeneration`/stale-invoice tracking, student-level credit-balance-only changes with no invoice `needs_resend` flag, and `activateTerm`'s implicit close not showing the informational panel (see below).
- [x] **Staff-child discount policy scope + default — shipped 2026-09-22, corrected 2026-09-23.** The 2026-09-22 build first gave every non-sibling category (`scholarship`, `bursary`, `financial_hardship`, `fee_waiver`, `other`) a fixed school-wide default on the Discount policy page and stripped `RequestDiscountModal.tsx` down to category-only selection for all of them. Owner reverted that same day: those categories genuinely vary per student/circumstance (a scholarship isn't the same % for everyone), so there's no sense in a school-wide fixed value for them — only `staff_child` is meant to be one fixed benefit for any staff member's child. **Final shipped behaviour:** `staff_child` alone reads its rate from Discount policy (`staffDiscountDefaultPct`, now defaulting to **25%**) and its scope (`staffDiscountScope: 'discountable_only' | 'full_invoice'`, now defaulting to **`full_invoice`** — "overall invoice" in the UI) — `RequestDiscountModal.tsx` shows it as a read-only "Applies" box, no amount input. Every other category is back to free input (amount, %/₦ unit, recurring toggle) at request time, exactly as before the 09-22 build — `resolveDiscountAmount()` (`money/invoices/[id]/discountActions.ts`) branches on `category === 'staff_child'` only; everything else validates and uses the requester's own input. Removed entirely: `CategoryDefault`/`OtherRequestableCategory` types, `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_DEFAULTS`, the "Other discounts" policy row, and all `categoryDefaults` plumbing across `queries/discounts.ts`, `discountActions.ts`, `RequestDiscountModal.tsx`, `school/discounts/actions.ts`, and `DiscountSettingsForm.tsx` (verified zero remaining references via grep). `computeDiscountsForInvoice`'s `staff_child`-only subtotal-vs-discountableSubtotal branch is unchanged from the 09-22 build. The live "Paystack Test School" test data was updated through the UI to match (Staff children: 25% off · overall invoice, saved and confirmed in the audit log), not just the code-level default. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Verified live end-to-end post-correction: Discount policy page shows no "Other discounts" row; Staff-child request shows the fixed "25% off the full invoice" read-only box; switching category to Scholarship in the same modal shows the free Amount/%-₦/Recurring fields again; submitted a real 15% scholarship request on a live invoice — auto-approved under the ₦30,000 threshold, landed as ₦7,500 (15% of the ₦50,000 discountable subtotal, correctly excluding the ₦10,000 non-discountable exam fee), confirmed on the invoice's discount total and timeline.

- [x] **Users & Roles — per-row actions consolidated into one "Manage" drawer — shipped 2026-09-23.** `UsersManager.tsx`'s Team & Trust table had a separate Reset password / Change email / Deactivate / Role-select control cluttering every row. Confirmed already-compliant rather than needing rework: the withdraw-student destructive-confirmation dialog (`StudentSettingsTab.tsx`'s `WithdrawConfirmModal`) — the owner's attached "NOW" screenshot was the mockup canvas's own before-illustration of the pre-redesign app, not a screenshot of the current build, which already matched the "NEXT" spec exactly (itemized ruled consequence rows, split outcome buttons, no hidden default). **`composeInvite.ts` rewritten** to the Modernist "Staff invite" email pattern from `Messages.dc.html` (FEES101 wordmark + red rule instead of the old navy/mint header, ink-on-ink no-radius button, ochre for anything needing attention, ink footer band) for the 5 functions that are genuinely live (`composeLockoutEmail`, `composeInviteExpiredEmail`, `composeEmailChangedToNewAddress`/`ToOldAddress`, `composeSchoolEmailVerification`). **Decided and shipped 2026-09-24:** switched `addStaff()`/`resendInvite()` (`team/users/actions.ts`) from `inviteUserByEmail()` to `generateLink({ type: 'invite' })` + `sendEmail()` through the existing Brevo path, so the real invite email is now this repo's own `composeInviteEmail()` template (already restyled above) instead of Supabase's dashboard-configured "Invite user" template. Picked over editing the dashboard template because the branded template already existed and was sitting unused, the Brevo send path is already production-proven for receipts/invoices/reminders, and it gets a `message_logs` audit trail for free (`messageType: 'invite'`, already a valid DB-constraint value). Verified `generateLink()`'s `action_link` against a throwaway test user via the raw Auth Admin REST API — same `.../auth/v1/verify?token=...&type=invite&redirect_to=...` format `inviteUserByEmail()` always produced, so it flows through the existing `/auth/callback` → `/set-password` handling unchanged; throwaway user deleted after. Not live-email-verified (this dev environment has `EMAIL_MODE=live`, so an actual send was deliberately not triggered against a disposable address) — owner should do one real invite/resend click-through to confirm the email arrives and renders correctly. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Left `--color-navy`/`--color-mint`/`--color-mint-light` tokens in `globals.css` alone for now (cosmetic-only cleanup, blocked on deciding the item above since `composeInviteEmail` — dead or not — is still technically a consumer).
- [x] **Blank "Fees this term" panel — investigated, partial fix shipped 2026-09-23.** Owner reported a student with many siblings sometimes shows a blank fee-structure panel, blocking opt-in management. A background investigation agent plus a live re-test (fetched all 29 real students in the reported test family — `PWTest_Stu_Bulk001-030` — directly) found **zero reproductions**: every one of the 29 detail pages rendered "Fees this term" correctly. Root mechanism confirmed either way: `students/[id]/page.tsx` gated the entire fee panel on a bare `{feesData && (...)}` with no fallback, so *any* falsy return from `getStudentFees()` — for any reason, now or in the future — would silently render nothing with no error, exactly matching the reported symptom (header + siblings sidebar, which come from separate queries, would still render fine). Fixed: the panel now always renders its heading and shows an explicit "Couldn't load this student's fees" message when `feesData` is null, instead of vanishing. Also code-reviewed `computeSiblingDiscount`'s per-family siblings query (`compute.ts`) — it's unbounded (no `.limit()`, unlike the sidebar list's own `SIBLINGS_LIMIT=20`), a plausible slow-path as a family's sibling count grows, but capping it would silently break sibling-discount-tier correctness for any family past the cap (rank lookup would miss later siblings entirely) — **not changed, flagged rather than blindly fixed**, since it needs a real limit-aware rank calculation, not just an added `.limit()`. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
- [x] **Siblings-list layout bug fixed — shipped 2026-09-23.** Owner clarified the real "siblings" complaint: not a crash, a layout bug — a family with ~20 siblings made the right-column Siblings panel far taller than the left column, and since `.m-2col-profile` is a CSS grid, a grid row's height always follows its *tallest* column regardless of `align-items` on the children. "Fees this term" was rendered as a full-width block *after* the grid closed, so it got pushed below whichever column was taller — for a big family, that meant a long blank gap and a long scroll before the fee/opt-in panel was even visible. Fixed three ways per the owner's own suggestions: (1) moved "Fees this term" to be the second panel *inside* the left column instead of a sibling after the grid, so it now always renders immediately under "Every term, every payment" regardless of the right column's height; (2) capped the Siblings list itself to a `420px` internal scroll area (`overflow-y-auto`, inline `style={{ maxHeight: 420 }}` — the Tailwind arbitrary-bracket class `max-h-[420px]` silently failed to compile under the WASM Tailwind JIT, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`, so used the established inline-style workaround instead); (3) the Siblings panel caption now states the family size as a short number ("19 others in this family, and where they stand this term.") instead of only being discoverable by scrolling. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. Verified live via CDP screenshots before/after — "Fees this term" now renders with no gap and the Siblings box is visibly capped/scrollable instead of stretching the whole right column.
- [x] **Reset-password flow rebuilt to match the Messages.dc.html staff-invite spec — shipped 2026-09-23.** `/set-password` and `/forgot-password` had no FEES101 branding at all — re-reading the live files found the earlier "already compliant" note from a prior session was wrong. Both now carry the mockup's wordmark (live text "FEES101" + a 24×2px red rule, not an image — nothing to fail to load). `/set-password`'s success screen was rebuilt to match the mockup's landing-screen spec: personalized heading ("Set a password, {FirstName}." when a name is available, from invite metadata or, for a self-service reset, a `users.name` lookup), a bordered SCHOOL / INVITED BY / ROLE info grid (renders only the fields actually present — a self-service reset has no inviter/role, so it shows just SCHOOL), a 4-segment password-strength bar (visual only, length-based — the enforced rule stays the 10-character minimum), and a new Confirm-password field with a mismatch check before submit. Deliberately left out two mockup lines that aren't actually true today: the "cannot be your name/email/school name" helper text (not validated) and the "recorded in the audit log" footer (no `logAuditEvent` call in this flow) — adding either would be misleading UI. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. Verified live: wordmark confirmed on `/forgot-password` (fresh incognito) and on `/set-password`'s expired-link branch (both incognito and the main session, whose auth token had separately expired mid-session — confirmed via a `/today` redirect-to-login check, unrelated to this change). **Not yet verified live:** the personalized success-branch heading/info-grid/strength-bar, since verifying it needs a session with a real, unexpired invite or reset link — code was reviewed closely against the spec and against the already-working invite-metadata branch instead.
- [x] **Manage opt-ins drawer rebuilt with real server-side search/pagination/class-filter — shipped & browser-verified 2026-09-23.** `ManageOptInsPanel.tsx` previously fetched every eligible student for a fee (group) in one shot and filtered client-side — fine at small scale, not at a few hundred students per class. Rebuilt around three server actions in `fees/structure/actions.ts`: `getOptInsSummaryForFeeGroup` (one-time initial load — total eligible count + already-opted-in IDs, so the running "N opted in of M" total and ₦ figure are correct across the whole set, not just the visible page), `getEligibleClassesForFeeGroup` (class-filter dropdown options), and `getOptInsPageForFeeGroup` (paginated, search- and class-filtered page fetch, `PER_PAGE=30`). Selection state (`selectedIds: Set<string>`) is sourced once on initial load then only added to/removed from locally as the admin ticks boxes across pages/searches/filters — never blown away by a page turn. `Select all`/`Clear` operate against the full filtered set via a dedicated `fetchMatchingIds()` call, not just the visible 30 rows. This supersedes the narrower single-item-fetch architecture the 2026-09-16 stress-test fix (`ManageOptInsPanel` "Select all" under a search filter, below) was built against — the StrictMode double-invoke race that fix guarded against no longer applies to this data-fetching shape, but the same "don't let a stale response clobber a newer one" discipline carried forward into the new page-fetch effect. **Fully browser-verified end-to-end** against a 70-student, 3-class opt-in fee: search-isolate a single student and toggle their opt-in, clear search, switch the class filter, page through all 3 pages (30/30/10, correct Prev/Next disabled states at both boundaries), Save, then close and reopen the drawer from a fresh page load to confirm the selection persisted server-side (`bulkUpdateOptInsForGroup`) and the fee's Gross Potential total updated to match (+₦15,000 for the one opt-in). Running total and ₦ figure stayed correct across every step. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. **Not committed.**
- [x] **Found and fixed a real data-loss bug in the Manage opt-ins drawer while verifying the rework above — shipped 2026-09-23.** `FeeStructureLayout.tsx` (the drawer's only caller) was passing `feeItemIds={managingOptInsGroup.items.map(i => i.id)}` and `groupItems={...map(...)}` as inline `.map()` calls directly in JSX — a fresh array reference on every single render of `FeeStructureLayout`, regardless of content. Since `feeItemIds` sits in `ManageOptInsPanel`'s initial-load `useEffect` dependency array, *any* unrelated re-render of the parent while the drawer was open (a toast, an error, `copying`/`bulkDeleteBusy` state, a route/searchParams change) made React see it as "changed" and re-fire the initial load — silently resetting `selectedIds` back to the server's last-saved state and wiping whatever the admin had just ticked, with no warning. This exactly explained two earlier "one-off dev-server hiccup" observations (a search-clear reset and a class-filter reset, both previously shrugged off) — both were this same bug, just not reproduced every time since it depends on an unrelated re-render coinciding with the moment of the action. **Fixed** by wrapping both derived arrays in `useMemo` keyed on `managingOptInsGroup` (`src/components/fees/FeeStructureLayout.tsx`), so they only get new references when the drawer is actually opened/closed for a different fee group. Re-verified against the exact repro: opted a student in, cleared search, set a class filter, then switched back to "All classes" (the precise sequence that used to wipe the selection) — selection now correctly survives. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. **Not committed.**
- [x] **Destructive-confirmation dialogs standardized on one itemized shell across the app — shipped 2026-09-23.** Several destructive/irreversible actions (deactivate a user, deactivate a class, remove a fee exemption, delete a fee group, cancel a term, and others) were still using the generic message-only `ConfirmDialog` or, in places, no structured confirmation at all — inconsistent with the canonical pattern already established by the withdraw-student dialog (`StudentSettingsTab.tsx`'s `WithdrawConfirmModal`: the action spelled out in words, concrete consequences itemized as ruled rows, every outcome its own button, no hidden default, no browser `confirm()`). New shared `src/components/ui/DestructiveConfirmModal.tsx` codifies that shell (eyebrow/title/description, itemized `rows`, trailing `note`, per-action buttons with `outline`/`danger`/`primary` variants) and is now wired into 12 surfaces: `FeeStructureLayout`, `CycleDetailLayout`, `CyclesLayout`, `EditFeeGroupPanel`, `YearEndRolloverWizard`, `InvoiceDetailLayout`, `UsersManager`, `StudentFeesTab`, and the academic-structure `ClassesTable`/`EditClassPanel`/`ManageSectionsModal`/`SessionsTab`. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. **Manually browser-tested and confirmed working by the owner (2026-09-23)** across the wired surfaces — not committed.
- [x] **`NoWidgetsFallback` (Today's narrow-role state) restyled to the app's own ink/paper idiom — shipped 2026-09-23.** Carried-forward backlog item from Phase 08 ("every empty/error/permission-denied state") — the component was still on the pre-redesign generic Tailwind look (`--color-surface` fill, `border-neutral-300` card grid) noted at the time it first shipped as "may look scanty... revisit once final UI/UX pass happens." No App Shell canvas state exists for this exact scenario (a role with zero dashboard-widget permissions), so rebuilt against the closest established precedent already in this exact file — the "Needs you" panel immediately above it on the same page (`today/page.tsx`): `.m-panel` (2px ink top rule), a `text-[22px] font-extrabold` heading, `.m-row` link rows (`grid`/`gap-4`/`py-3.5`/hover-surface) instead of a card grid, matching the "Nothing is waiting on you" empty-state copy convention already used on the same page. Copy tightened to name what's missing before showing what's reachable, per the [[redesign-replicate-not-approximate]] empty-state philosophy ("names what is missing, why it matters, and the single next action"). `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. **Not live-screenshot-verified** — no existing test persona holds zero dashboard-widget permissions (Bursar/Accounts clerk/Class teacher test personas all carry at least one), so reaching this state needs a custom no-permission role created first; reasoned correct against the file's own established sibling pattern instead. **Not committed.**
- [x] **Student Fees tab unified into one continuous ledger — shipped 2026-09-24.** The last redesign gap on the student profile: `StudentFeesTab.tsx` (embedded as the "Fees this term" panel in `students/[id]/page.tsx`, itself already merged into one surface by an earlier pass) still showed three separate blocks — a "Required fees" table with its own header/footer, a separate "Optional fees" table, and a standalone `bg-[var(--color-ink)]` "Expected bill" summary box below both — instead of the canvas's one-continuous-ledger pattern (`App Shell.dc.html`'s `stuItems` data, tagged with a `kind` label, followed by bold total rows in the same block). Replaced with one `.m-table` under a single "Fees ledger" heading (Item / Kind / Amount / Status columns), grouped by uppercase divider rows — Required (auto-applied fees, exemption actions), Opt-in (checkbox toggle, unchanged logic, now labelled in the Kind column), Adjustments (discount + credit-applied rows, using `data.expectedCreditApplied` — the portion actually netted into this term's bill — not the student's total `creditBalance`, which can be larger and would read inconsistently against the footer total) — and a single bold `<tfoot>` row for "Expected bill this term." No handler/modal logic touched, only the display markup. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. Verified live via CDP against two real students: one with only required fees (confirms the Required group + footer), one with opt-in/discount/credit data (confirms the Adjustments group, a "Credit applied" line-wrap cosmetic bug fixed with `whitespace-nowrap`, and the ochre "Update invoice" variant still rendering correctly). Re-verified the restructured "Mark exempt" action still opens its dialog correctly against the new markup (cancelled without submitting, to avoid leaving test data on a real-looking student record).
- [x] **Staff-invite email now sent through this app's own branded template, not Supabase's dashboard one — shipped 2026-09-24.** See the decision write-up above (line ~60) — `addStaff()`/`resendInvite()` switched from `inviteUserByEmail()` to `generateLink()` + `sendEmail()`, so `composeInviteEmail()`'s Modernist styling (FEES101 wordmark, ink button, ochre/red accents) is now what a real invitee actually receives, sent via the same Brevo path already proven for receipts/invoices/reminders, with a `message_logs` row (`messageType: 'invite'`) for the first time. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. `generateLink()`'s output verified against a throwaway test user via the raw Auth Admin REST API (correct `action_link` format, cleaned up after) rather than triggering a real send, since this dev environment's `EMAIL_MODE=live`. **Owner confirmed 2026-09-24: sent a real test invite and it arrived correctly.** Also confirmed live that the restructured Fees ledger's "Mark exempt" action still works.
- [x] **Bulk "Send" on the invoices list now actually clears the dashboard's "N invoices changed and not resent" backlog — shipped 2026-09-24.** Owner saw the `/today` dashboard's "Needs you" panel (already-shipped indicator, see Feature A1 history above) showing "53 invoices changed and not resent" and assumed clicking through and using the list page's bulk-send button already resolved it. It didn't, for a reason invisible from the UI: `startBulkSendInvoicesJob` (`sendInvoice.ts`) and `getAllInvoicesForList`'s `needsSend` count (`fees.ts`) both required `outstanding_amount > 0` before an invoice qualified as "needs sending" — a rule that makes sense for a never-sent invoice, but silently excluded `needs_resend = true` invoices that had already been fully paid and then changed (fee items edited after payment). The dashboard's own count has no such balance check (correctly — a parent needs to know an invoice changed even if it nets to zero owed), so it could show a backlog that the bulk-send button, its count, and the job underneath it would never actually pick up, no matter how many times it ran. Fixed by making `needs_resend = true` bypass the balance gate in both places — `sendInvoice.ts`'s query changed from `.gt('outstanding_amount', 0).or('sent_at.is.null,needs_resend.eq.true')` to `.or('and(sent_at.is.null,outstanding_amount.gt.0),needs_resend.eq.true')`, and `fees.ts`'s `needsSend` count given the matching `i.needsResend || (!i.sentAt && i.outstandingAmount > 0)` shape — so the job and the count now agree with each other and with the dashboard's definition of "needs resend." Also relabelled the button from always "Send N unsent" to "Send N pending" whenever any needs-resend invoices are in the mix, so it doesn't overclaim. No new UI was built — this reuses the existing `BulkSendInvoicesPanel` progress/loading treatment as-is (per Foundations), unchanged. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. Not yet live-verified against a real fully-paid-then-edited invoice — owner should click "Send" from the invoices list (or via the dashboard's "Needs you" link) and confirm the count matches the dashboard's and drops to zero after the job completes.
- [x] **Dashboard "Needs you" resend row is now a direct action, not just a link — shipped 2026-09-24.** Owner's follow-up after testing the above: from the dashboard, the "N invoices changed and not resent" row only linked through to the filtered invoices list — clearing the backlog still took an extra trip. Turned that one row into a "Resend now or review?" choice: clicking it opens a small modal (new `NeedsYouList.tsx` client component, replacing the plain `<Link>` row map in `today/page.tsx`) with "Review" (same link-through as before, unchanged) and "Resend now" (kicks off the bulk-send job immediately, using `BulkSendInvoicesPanel` — the same "regular loading way" progress UI already used on the invoices list, unchanged component). Only this one row is interactive this way; overdue/discounts/unbilled rows are still plain links, since resending is the only one of the four with a real one-click resolution. Scoped the job to needs-resend-only for this entry point so the modal's count stays honest and matches exactly what the dashboard promised (rather than silently also sweeping up unrelated never-sent invoices): added an `onlyNeedsResend` option to `startBulkSendInvoicesJob` (`sendInvoice.ts`) and `startBulkSend` (`invoices/actions.ts`), and a `skipConfirm` option to `BulkSendInvoicesPanel` so clicking "Resend now" (already the confirmation) goes straight to the progress bar instead of asking to confirm a second time. Follow-up from the owner after seeing the modal: the row should show the send progress itself, not just the popup, so a glance at the dashboard is enough without the panel staying open. `NeedsYouList.tsx` now reads the same tracked job (`useActiveJobs`/`useTrackedJob`, scoped to `jobType === 'bulk_send'`) the floating progress chip and `BulkSendInvoicesPanel` already use, so while a send is running the row itself swaps its "NEEDS RESEND" label and subtitle for a live "Sending X/Y" count and a thin progress bar — genuinely tethered to the real job, not a local flag, so it stays lit even if the panel is closed, or if the send was started from the invoices list page instead. Clicking the row again while a send is running reopens that job's own progress panel directly, skipping the choice modal since the decision's already made. Reverts to the normal "NEEDS RESEND" row the moment the job ends. `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. Live-verified via CDP: dashboard row correctly renders live data, the choice modal opens and its "Resend now" skips straight to `BulkSendInvoicesPanel`'s progress view (confirming `skipConfirm`), the row shows "Sending 0/53" with a progress bar synced to the same floating tracker while the job runs, and reverts to "NEEDS RESEND" once the job completes. **Caveat observed on this dev database, not a defect in this feature:** most of the 53 flagged invoices belong to demo/test students with no payment account provisioned yet, so their sends fail with "No payment account provisioned for this student yet." and correctly leave `needs_resend` set (only a successful send clears it) — meaning the count won't visibly drop to zero here. Worth a real test against a student with a valid phone/email/payment account to see it clear end to end.

## 🧪 Testing checklist — pull & verify (fee-change / invoicing work, Sep 2026)

> Running list of what to browser-test after a pull, so nothing built goes unverified. Test data: two test schools (Paystack + Monnify), fresh current-dated terms (First Term active, Second/Third draft), 6 students each with DVAs. Check items off as you confirm them; note any that break.

**✅ Verified working (this cycle):**
- [x] **Generate First Term invoices** — 6 students × ₦50k, per-cycle generation via background job (Test 1)
- [x] **Bulk "Send all" + resume** — send job runs, cancel/resume mid-way, no loop-on-failure (Test 2)
- [x] **Opt-in on a paid invoice** — additive engine appends the fee to a live/paid invoice instantly, recomputes outstanding/status; no clawback (Test 3 · `004f4e3`, `c4ab1b8`)
- [x] **Undo a mistaken opt-in on a paid invoice (safe removal)** — opt-out then "Update invoice" allowed because the new total still covers what's paid (clawback-only lock); banner + toast correct (Test 4 · `5e2b9a1`). **Actually verified in-browser 2026-09-15.**
- [x] **Opt-in/opt-out UX (toggle, confirm, toast, send-confirm)** — toggle switch, confirm dialog on opt-in, toast on both, confirm before parent-facing send (`6194d6f`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 5).**
- [x] **True clawback is hard-blocked** — opt-out/regenerate blocked with the manual refund/credit reconciliation message on a would-be clawback; invoice unchanged (`5e2b9a1`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 6).**
- [x] **Opt-out on a truly-locked PAID invoice → credit choice** — locked case offers defer-to-next-term + Credit/Leave-as-is dialog (`ed45f9f`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 7).**
- [x] **Send a receipt on a fully-paid invoice** — `paid` invoice's button reads "Send receipt" and sends a real receipt, not a ₦0 invoice (`b4e6015`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 8).**
- [x] **Recurring vs one-time optional fee** — one-time optional fee does not reappear next term, recurring one does (`226b95b`, `2b8a5df`). **Verified 2026-09-15 (Test 8):** Yusuf opted into Bus (recurring) + Uniform (one-time) on Second Term, then a new "test term" created rolling forward from Second Term — backend confirms Bus opt-in carried, Uniform opt-in did NOT. (Note: the Fees tab is active-term-only, so the roll-forward result is confirmed via the new term's data/invoice, not the student Fees tab — see the cross-term viewing item below.)
- [x] **Sibling/discountable opt-in recompute** — opting a discounted student into a discountable optional fee applies their discount share correctly (`36b5167`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 9).**
- [x] **Close First Term → carry-forward** — close with outstanding balances, balance carries into next term's invoice, runs as a job with live progress at scale (`closeTermAndCarryForward`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 9).**
- [x] **Closed-term resend / carry-forward display** — carried invoice with a successor blocks resend and names the destination; no-successor closed invoice stays sendable (`b6fe6d1`, `dfdfbb8`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 9).**
- [x] **Request discount blocked on a superseded closed invoice** — blocked (successor named) on a carried closed invoice, allowed on a no-successor one (`726d277`). **Confirmed by owner in-browser 2026-09-15 (Test 9).**

**⬜ Still to test:**
- [ ] **Regenerate-all progress accuracy** — "Regenerate all" targets only stale invoices and reports correct N/N (no more "regenerated 1/5" when only 1 was stale) (`b82dd01`). **Code-reviewed 2026-09-15: traced the exact 5-invoice/1-locked/1-stale/3-current scenario end to end, `total`/`lockedCount` both computed and reported correctly — correct as-is.** Still needs an actual in-browser click-through to fully confirm (needs a fee/discount cut *after* full payment to actually exercise the locked-count path, not just any sent/paid invoice).
- [ ] **Logout / switch users** — after logout the session is actually cleared so a different user can sign in (`7d9ffcf`). **Code-reviewed 2026-09-15: `auth.signOut()` + cookie clearing in the route handler is correct, and every permission check re-validates per-request (`getClaims()`), so no stale-session risk — correct as-is.** Still needs an actual in-browser click-through to confirm.

**🆕 New gap found while testing (2026-09-15):**
- [x] **`ActiveJobsProvider`'s localStorage job-tracking key isn't cleared on logout or scoped per user** — found while code-reviewing the logout item above. `fees101_active_jobs` (`src/lib/jobs/ActiveJobsProvider.tsx:79`) is a single unscoped `localStorage` key that gets replayed on every mount regardless of who's signed in, and logout never clears it. If a second user logs into the same browser tab right after the first logs out (same machine, e.g. shared front-desk computer), they can briefly see the first user's in-flight job chip (cycle name, progress) until it completes or a cross-school lookup fails server-side (`job.school_id !== ctx.schoolId` is already enforced, so no actual data leaks — just the label/progress UI). Low severity, no financial/student data exposed, but should clear that key in the logout route handler. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** the actual sign-out route (`/logout`) is a server route handler with no access to `localStorage`, so the clear happens where the logout click originates instead — `UserMenu.tsx`'s `handleLogout` now removes the key (exported as `ACTIVE_JOBS_STORAGE_KEY` from `ActiveJobsProvider.tsx` so there's one source of truth for the name) right after the `/logout` fetch resolves, before navigating to `/login`. Not extended to the separate `GET /logout` auto-bounce path (deactivated/scheduled-for-deletion accounts, triggered by a server redirect with no client JS in the loop) — that's a rarer edge case stacked on an already-low-severity, already-server-validated gap, and not worth a second mechanism for.
- [ ] **Withdrawn/graduated student's stray open invoice — cancel-or-leave-open flow** — see the full test steps written under this feature further down in the roadmap (withdraw with unpaid invoice → cancel it; withdraw with a part-paid/credited invoice → manual-review warning, direct cancel refused; standalone "Cancel invoice" button on `/invoices/[id]`; dashboard/fees-overview/cycle totals exclude the cancelled amount but keep counting it)

**🆕 New gaps found during full-app parallel stress-test (2026-09-16, 13 background agents — functional + adversarial/loophole + webhook testing):**
- [x] **`ManageOptInsPanel` "Select all" silently loses the selection under a search filter — root cause confirmed** — `src/components/fees/ManageOptInsPanel.tsx`'s data-load `useEffect` calls `getOptInsForFeeItem` with no `AbortController`/ignore-flag/request-generation guard. `next.config.ts` has no explicit `reactStrictMode`, so Next defaults it to `true` in dev (`__NEXT_STRICT_MODE_APP` — confirmed in `node_modules/next/dist/build/define-env.js`), which double-invokes the effect on mount. The first response resolves, flips `loading` to false, and reveals the interactive picker; the second, still-in-flight duplicate can resolve later and unconditionally calls `setSelectedIds(new Set(result.optedInStudentIds))` with the *original* (still-empty) set, silently clobbering a "Select all" click made in between. Not a filter-logic bug — it's a timing race, and typing into the search box before clicking just widens the window for the stale response to land after the click, which is why it reproduces far more reliably with a filter applied. Fix: guard the effect with an `ignore`/`AbortController` flag (standard React fix for double-invoked async effects under StrictMode) so a stale response never overwrites state after a newer one (or a user action) has already landed. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** added the `ignore` flag exactly as prescribed — set `true` in the effect's cleanup function, checked immediately after each `await` before touching `students`/`selectedIds`/`loading` state, in both the single-item and group branches. A stale duplicate invocation (StrictMode double-mount, or a fast prop change re-firing the effect) now silently drops its result instead of overwriting whatever the current/newer load or a user's "Select all" click already set.
- [x] **`addStudent()` family-matching has no secondary identity check and never syncs the reused family's name** — `src/app/(app)/students/actions.ts`: matches an existing family by exact `primary_parent_phone` string equality only (`.eq('primary_parent_phone', input.primaryParentPhone)`) — no normalization (spacing/leading-zero/country-code variants), no case-insensitivity, and no secondary check against name/email/address. A second family entering the same phone number with a different or misspelled parent name silently attaches to the first family's record and the stored `primary_parent_name`/email are never updated on reuse, so the family record can drift out of sync with what later guardians actually typed. Needs: normalize the phone before matching, and either warn staff on a name mismatch or update the stored name/email when a match is reused. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** both `addStudent()` and `processCsvImportChunk()` now match and store phones through the existing `normalizePhone` (`lib/messaging/sendMessage.ts`), so "0803...", "+234 803...", and "234803..." all resolve to the same family instead of fragmenting into separate records. Picked the "warn staff" half of the fix (not silent name/email sync — auto-overwriting a stored name on every reuse could itself be abused): `addStudent()` now returns a `needsConfirmation` result with the existing family's name whenever a phone match's parent name doesn't match, and `AddStudentModal.tsx` shows a confirm dialog ("Same family as an existing parent?") before linking — confirmed via a new `confirmFamilyLink` param. CSV bulk import has no one to prompt mid-batch, so a name-mismatched row is instead pulled out and pushed into the existing `failedRows` review mechanism with a reason explaining the conflict, rather than silently merging. Note: family phone numbers stored *before* this fix keep whatever raw format they were entered in — no retroactive backfill was run (a merge across differently-formatted duplicates of the same real number risks colliding with the `families_school_phone` unique index and needs a careful one-off migration, not an automatic one) — new entries will still fail to match an old, differently-formatted number until that family's row happens to get touched again.
- [x] **`/discounts` Approve button has no confirmation dialog — double-approval collision risk** — found via two sibling stress-test agents approving the same pending request within the same second; the action applies instantly with zero confirm step, so two approvers (or one approver double-clicking on a slow connection) can both fire the approval before either UI updates, risking a duplicate-apply race on the discount. Add a confirm step or an optimistic-disable-on-click guard, and verify the server action itself is idempotent against a duplicate approval of the same request. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** duplicate of the finding below — see that entry for the UI fix. The server-action idempotency half is also done: `approveDiscount`'s atomic claim-then-act guard (see the double-approval race finding above) makes a duplicate approval fail cleanly instead of double-applying, independent of the UI fix.
- [x] **Opt-out clawback "Leave as-is" branch leaves a permanent unresolved credit with no refund/reminder trail** — when a truly-locked paid invoice's opt-out clawback dialog is resolved via "Leave as-is" (as opposed to "defer to next term" or "credit"), the resulting overpayment (confirmed via a live repro: ₦20,000) is left sitting as a permanently unresolved credit with nothing tracking it for refund or follow-up — no reminder, no flag, no surfaced balance anywhere staff would see it again. Needs a persisted "unresolved credit" marker and a surface (student profile or a dedicated report) where staff can find and resolve these later. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** deliberately did not reuse `students.credit_balance` — that field auto-applies to the student's next invoice, which is the opposite of "leave as-is" (the dialog's own copy already says "Cash refunds aren't handled here — contact support to reconcile those", i.e. this is meant to represent money owed back to the family outside the app, not an automatic future-fee discount). New table `db/unresolved_credits.sql` (school-scoped RLS, needs a one-time run in the Supabase SQL editor) gives each occurrence a real row instead of just an audit-log line. `resolveDeferredOptOutOverage`'s "leave" branch now inserts one; a new `resolveUnresolvedCredit` action marks it resolved. Surfaced in two places: an amber banner at the top of the student's Fees tab (`StudentFeesTab.tsx`) listing every open one for that student with a "Mark as resolved" button, and a new "Unresolved credits" report (`src/lib/reports/reports.ts` + `ReportsLayout.tsx`, gated behind `see-financial-totals` like the other money reports) so staff can see every school's open-and-resolved history in one CSV without visiting each student individually.
- [x] **`setStudentExemption()` silently permanently locks an invoice with no staff-facing warning** — exempting a student from a fee that's already reflected on a sent/paid invoice locks that invoice going forward with no warning shown to the staff member making the change at the point of action — they only discover it later when trying to regenerate. Add an inline warning in the exemption UI when it's about to affect an already-sent invoice. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `setStudentExemption` itself never touches the invoice (it only writes a `student_fee_adjustments` row) — the actual lock is `regenerateInvoice`'s existing clawback guard (`fees/cycles/actions.ts`) refusing to recompute once the new total would drop below what's already paid, mirrored client-side by `StudentFeesTab.tsx`'s `isLocked`. Added the missing warning at the one place staff actually act — the "Mark as exempt" dialog now projects `expectedBill − thisFee.amount` against `existingInvoice.paidAmount` and shows an inline amber banner naming the amount paid and explaining the invoice will lock at its current amount pending manual refund/credit reconciliation, whenever that exemption would trigger the clawback lock. A second, milder banner covers the sent-but-unpaid case (invoice already shown to the parent with this fee included) prompting a manual notify after regenerating. Both banners are purely informational — the exemption still proceeds either way, matching how every other lock warning in this codebase (e.g. the opt-out overage dialog) is handled as "inform, don't block."
- [x] **One-time fee items are never dropped on term roll-forward (manual AND year-end rollover) — contradicts the product's own UI copy** — `createTerm`'s fee-copy block (`src/app/(app)/fees/cycles/actions.ts:276-298`) copies every fee item from the source cycle with no `is_recurring` filter; `continueYearEndRollover` reuses this same function, so a one-time fee (e.g. "Excursion Fee") reappears on every subsequent term forever via both paths. Directly contradicts `FeeFormPanel.tsx`'s own explanatory copy ("Billed once, then automatically drops off future terms"). Confirmed live via DB query on a real year-end rollover, not just the UI. Fix: filter `sourceFees` to `is_recurring !== false` before copying. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** added `.eq('is_recurring', true)` to `createTerm`'s `sourceFees` select, shared by both manual roll-forward and year-end rollover (`continueYearEndRollover` calls `createTerm`), so a one-time fee no longer reappears on any subsequently created term.
- [x] **Re-activating an already-active term self-closes-then-reactivates instead of no-op'ing** — `activateTerm` (`src/app/(app)/fees/cycles/actions.ts:540`) unconditionally closes "whatever the current active cycle is" (looked up fresh at execution time) and then activates the target, with no guard for "target is already the active term." Confirmed via a stale-tab double-activate race: audit log shows the term getting `term.closed_carried_forward` against **itself**, then `term.activated` again, seconds after it was already correctly activated. In this repro the term had 0 invoices so no data was corrupted, but on a term with real outstanding balances this would run the carry-forward job against the term's own invoices. `closeTerm` already has the correct "already in target state" guard (confirmed: closing an already-closed term is cleanly rejected with zero duplicate audit entries) — `activateTerm` needs the same guard. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `activateTerm` now selects the target term's own `status` and returns `{ error: 'This term is already active' }` immediately if it's already `active`, mirroring `closeTerm`'s existing "already in target state" guard — closes off the self-referential close-then-reactivate race outright.
- [x] **Manual "Roll forward fees from" does not carry forward student-level fee opt-ins, but year-end rollover does — same stated feature, inconsistent fidelity** — confirmed live: a student's opt-in to an optional fee did not survive manual term roll-forward, but the equivalent opt-in (with `carry_forward:true`) correctly survived year-end rollover. Two mechanisms both described as "copy fees forward" silently differ in what they actually carry — likely to surprise an admin doing a manual mid-year roll-forward expecting year-end-rollover-level fidelity. Needs a product decision on whether manual roll-forward should also carry opt-ins, then align the two code paths. **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-16, left open:** the product decision is easy — they should align, and per the code they already do. `createTerm` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts:306-310`) calls the exact same shared helper, `carryForwardFeeAdjustments` (`lib/fees/carryForwardAdjustments.ts`), that `continueYearEndRollover` calls, gated only on `!form.skipAdjustmentCarryForward` — and `CreateTermPanel.tsx` (the manual "Roll forward fees from" UI, the only caller of manual `createTerm` with a `rollForwardFromCycleId`) never sets that flag, so the carry-forward call fires unconditionally on the manual path too, correctly ordered after the new term's fee items are created (needed since matching is by name against the *new* fee-item rows). No divergence found between the two paths by code review or git history — this shared-helper wiring predates this session's changes (from commit `0a292ee`, "Add year-end rollover wizard; harden discounts and opt-in carry-forward"). Left open rather than marked fixed since no code change was made and the original finding claims a live repro against presumably the same code — re-verify live during the full browser walkthrough; if it reproduces, look for a data-shape issue in the specific fee/class setup used (e.g. a name/class mismatch falling into the `unmatched` bucket). **FIXED 2026-09-16 (follow-up):** confirmed that last part was a real, separate gap — `createTerm` returned `unmatchedAdjustments` but `CreateTermPanel.tsx` dropped it on the floor, so a genuine name/class mismatch during manual roll-forward would silently lose a student's opt-in/exemption with zero indication to the admin (the exact "same feature, inconsistent fidelity" risk this finding worried about, just one level deeper — carry-forward *logic* was fine, but a real per-student match failure had no surface at all, on either path's manual entry point). Wired it through: `CreateTermPanel`'s `onSuccess` now passes `unmatchedAdjustments` up, `CyclesLayout.tsx` holds it in state and shows a dismissible amber banner ("N fee opt-in/exemption(s) couldn't be matched to a fee item in the new term and weren't carried forward"), mirroring the wording already used for the same data shape in `YearEndRolloverWizard.tsx` so both roll-forward entry points now report this consistently. The original "does manual roll-forward carry opt-ins at all" question is still left open per above, pending live re-verification. **RE-VERIFIED LIVE 2026-09-16 (final walkthrough phase), now closed:** ran a real year-end rollover end-to-end via the browser against "Greenfield Comprehensive Academy" and checked the new term's fee items and School Bus opt-in state directly — fee items (recurring ones) and student-level fee adjustments both came through correctly with no manual re-creation needed, no unmatched-adjustment warnings, and no divergence from what manual roll-forward's own shared-helper code path would do. No reproduction of the original "opt-in didn't survive" symptom. Treating the original finding as resolved by the shared-helper wiring described above, with this session's live run as the confirming re-test.
- [x] **Dashboard "Collected" KPI has no sanity bound and can silently exceed 100%** — `getDashboardKPIs`/`getCollectionByClass` (`src/lib/queries/dashboard.ts`) deliberately compute "Collected" as cash-received-by-date rather than invoice-allocation (intentional, documented in the code — a class or school that received more than a term's fees should be able to show over 100%, same as an individual overpaying student). Confirmed intentional design, not a bug, but flagging because a raw collection percentage well over 100% with no accompanying explanation can read as broken data to a school admin glancing at the dashboard — consider a small "includes prepayment for other terms" note when the percentage exceeds 100%. Live-confirmed how bad this can look with zero guardrail: one stray ₦9,999,999,999 test payment row (`invoice_id=null`) made a term's "Collected" figure display as ₦10,000,530,122.45 against ₦340,000 expected (2941%) — the formula reproduced the number exactly from the DB (not a calc bug), but nothing anywhere in the pipeline clamps or flags an obviously-anomalous figure before it hits the dashboard. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** picked the "explain, don't clamp" half — clamping or hiding an unusually high figure would hide a real anomaly (like the stray-payment repro above) from the people who need to see it. The top KPI card's percentage now grows a "(incl. prepayment/overpayment)" note plus a `title` tooltip whenever it exceeds 100%; `CollectionChart`'s per-class bullet and table views mark any over-100% row with a `*` and a matching footnote/tooltip explaining the same thing. The underlying anomalous-payment case is separately covered by the new admin-notification alerting added to `applyProviderPayment` (see the webhook sanity-cap finding below) — that's the actual guardrail against a stray figure going unnoticed; this fix is purely the display-side context so a legitimately-over-100% number doesn't read as broken.
- [x] **`credit_balance` is never applied when an invoice is updated via the fee opt-in ("additive") path — only on full invoice generation** — `src/lib/invoicing/addOptInLine.ts` (`applyOptInAdditionToLiveInvoice`) explicitly leaves `credit_applied`/`students.credit_balance` untouched by design, while `invoiceGeneration.ts` applies credit on full regeneration. Confirmed live: a student with ₦20,000 unapplied credit opted into a new ₦20,000 fee — the invoice recomputed the sibling discount correctly but showed `outstanding_amount: 16000` with `credit_applied` still `0` and the credit still sitting unused, directly contradicting the Payment History tab's own banner promise ("used automatically the next time an invoice is generated for this student"). Fix: apply available credit in the additive path too, or make the banner's wording conditional on which path last touched the invoice. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `applyOptInAdditionToLiveInvoice` now computes how much of the student's live `credit_balance` (whatever wasn't already spent on this invoice) can cover the newly-added fee — capped at both the live balance and the actual remaining unpaid amount, so it can never over-apply — adds a `Credit balance applied` line item, and writes the invoice update plus the credit-balance delta together through the existing `apply_invoice_recompute` RPC (the same atomic invoice+credit function used by full regeneration), instead of the old plain `.update()`. A crash between the two writes is no longer possible since they're one DB transaction. The Payment History tab's banner wording is now accurate for both paths, so no copy change was needed.
- [x] **Invoice Detail page never surfaces a student's unapplied credit balance, unlike the Payment History tab** — cross-page gap: `/invoices/{id}` shows only the invoice's own Outstanding figure with no mention that the student has credit sitting on account that could cover part or all of it, while the student's Payment History tab correctly shows a "₦X received but not yet applied" banner for the same student. Anyone working from the invoice page alone has no way to know unapplied credit exists. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `getInvoiceByIdForSchool`/`getInvoicesByCycleId` (`lib/queries/fees.ts`) now also select the student's live `credit_balance` and expose it on `InvoiceDetail` as `studentCreditBalance` (distinct from the invoice's own `creditApplied`); `InvoiceDetailLayout.tsx` shows the same "₦X of this student's credit balance is unapplied — it will be used automatically the next time an invoice is generated or updated" banner the Payment History tab already had, whenever it's greater than zero.
- [x] **A cancelled invoice can never be reissued for that term, even after the student is un-withdrawn back to Active — no admin-facing warning of this permanent state** — confirmed live: withdraw → cancel the surfaced open invoice → reactivate via Edit Details → Active. Fees tab is then permanently stuck on "Cancelled — reissuing not yet supported" with no "Generate invoice" option ever reappearing for that term, and nothing outside that one static tab message warns staff. Root enabler of the revenue-leak loophole below. Needs either a reissue path for a cancelled invoice on an active student, or a clear staff-facing flag distinguishing "genuinely cancelled, don't rebill" from "cancelled while withdrawn, now active again, needs a fresh invoice." **FIXED 2026-09-16:** picked option (b) from the sibling gap below — un-cancel and recompute the existing row (safe because `cancelInvoice` only ever allows cancelling when `paid_amount` and `credit_applied` are both zero, so there's never a clawback risk). Shared helper `regenerateCancelledInvoiceOnReactivation` (`students/[id]/actions.ts`) now runs automatically inside both `reactivateStudent` and `updateStudentDetails` whenever status moves back to `active`. For the "still active, invoice cancelled by mistake" case (no status change involved), new standalone action `regenerateCancelledInvoice` does the same thing, wired to a real "Generate new invoice" button on `StudentFeesTab.tsx` (replacing the old permanent "reissuing not yet supported" dead end) — gated on the student currently being `active`.
- [x] **Overview tab's "Generate invoice" button is dead (hardcoded `disabled`) while an identical-looking, fully working one exists on the Fees tab** — confirmed live via `isDisabled()` in the browser, not just source. A user landing on the default Overview tab of a student with no invoice yet will very likely try that button first, get no feedback at all, and conclude the app is broken before finding the working control one tab over. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** replaced the hardcoded-disabled placeholder with a new `GenerateInvoiceButton` component wired to the same existing `generateInvoiceForStudent` action the Fees tab already used — confirm dialog, permission gate (`manage-invoices`), and a disabled state with an accurate tooltip when there's no active billing cycle to generate against. Both tabs now go through the same real control instead of one being a dead stub.
- [x] **CSV import review screen's "Errors" stat tile is hardcoded to 0** — always shows zero even when the "Need attention" tile and the per-row table above it correctly show real validation errors. Cosmetic/non-blocking but confusing at a glance. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** tile now sums `row.errors.length` across all rows instead of a literal `0`.
- [x] **`StudentActivityTimeline` always renders "Invoice sent," even when `sent_at` is null** — `src/components/students/StudentActivityTimeline.tsx` line 69 hardcodes the "sent" description for every invoice event. Confirmed live on an invoice whose own DB row has `sent_at: NULL`, `status: 'pending'`, and whose own page still shows "Send invoice" as the primary action — the mini-timeline directly contradicts both the DB and the rest of the same page. **Cross-page inconsistency**: the global `/activity` feed gets this right (gates the same event type on `sent_at`); the student's own mini-timeline does not. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** the timeline's invoice query now also selects `sent_at`, and the description branches on it — `'Invoice sent to {parent}'` when `sent_at` is set, `'Invoice generated'` otherwise — matching the global `/activity` feed's existing behavior.
- [x] **Several audit-log action keys have no humanized label and leak the raw dotted string** — confirmed missing from `ACTION_LABELS` in `auditLogLabels.ts`: `invoice.cancelled`, `invoice.fee_added`, `invoice.receipt_sent` (seen live in both the UI table and the downloaded CSV — same `actionLabel()` fallback backs both, so the bug is consistent rather than screen-only) plus `student.opt_out_deferred_paid_invoice` and `student.opt_out_overage_resolved` (found via code, not yet seen live). **FIXED 2026-09-16:** all five keys added to `ACTION_LABELS`.
- [x] **Hydration mismatch on `/activity`** — server-rendered relative time ("5m ago") differs from the client re-render ("6m ago") for a table cell, throwing a recoverable but real React hydration warning caught via required pageerror listeners during stress-testing. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** the inline `timeAgo()` render moved into a new `RelativeTime` client component (`src/components/activity/RelativeTime.tsx`) that renders the stable absolute `formatDateTime` string until mounted, then swaps to the live relative string — server and first client paint now always agree.
- [x] **Confirmed (not new, but re-verified live): invoice cancellations, student status changes, and credit-balance adjustments are logged in the Audit Log but have no counterpart in the Activity Feed** — re-confirmed against real freshly-generated events during this stress-test (not just a structural SQL read): a cancelled invoice and a withdrawn student both appeared correctly in the Audit Log with zero trace in the Activity Feed's own category filters. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `db/activity_feed_add_missing_events.sql` adds three new branches to the `activity_feed` view, sourced from `audit_log` (the only place these events exist as discrete, timestamped rows — none of the three has a usable timestamp on its own domain table: an invoice's cancellation isn't distinguishable from any other update via `invoices.updated_at`, `students.updated_at` fires on any profile edit not just a status change, and `credit_balance` is a running total with no history table). `src/lib/queries/activity.ts`'s `describe()` gained matching cases for `invoice_cancelled`, `student_status_changed`, and `credit_balance_adjusted`. Credit-balance coverage is scoped to the "credit" decision from the deferred-opt-out overage flow specifically (the "leave" decision doesn't touch the balance, so it isn't an adjustment) — a more general credit-balance-adjustment event source doesn't exist yet; broader coverage is tracked separately below ("Why did my total change?"). **Needs `db/activity_feed_add_missing_events.sql` run manually in the Supabase SQL editor** — it's a `CREATE OR REPLACE` of the full view, supersedes `db/activity_feed_view.sql`.
- [x] **HIGH — Withdraw → cancel the surfaced open invoice → reactivate erases a full term's fee, repeatably, with no self-healing and no audit anomaly** — confirmed live end-to-end. `prepareInvoiceGeneration` treats any invoice row for that term (including a cancelled one) as "already invoiced" and skips the student on "Generate invoices"; `prepareInvoiceRegeneration` explicitly and permanently excludes `status='cancelled'` invoices from "Regenerate all." Together these make a withdrawn-then-reactivated student with a cancelled invoice a dead end — the school can never bill them for that term again by any UI action found, and the cycle-detail page's coverage tile ("Invoices N/N") and per-row badge give no visual distinction from a normal cancellation, so nothing flags it for staff review. Direct, repeatable revenue leakage. **FIXED 2026-09-16** — see the reissue fix above; reactivation now self-heals the cancelled invoice automatically instead of leaving a dead unbillable row.
- [x] **HIGH — No dedicated reactivate/un-withdraw flow exists anywhere; the only path is the generic Edit Details status dropdown, which runs zero business logic** — confirmed live: flipping status back to Active via Edit Student Details leaves `withdrawn_at` stale (never cleared) and performs no invoice reconciliation of any kind — it's a raw field write, not a real reactivation. This is the direct root enabler of the revenue-leak item above. Separately, the withdraw-confirmation modal's own copy claims "You can reverse this from the Settings tab later," which overstates what exists today (a generic details-edit dropdown, not a dedicated Settings-tab reactivation control) — worth fixing the copy alongside the functional gap. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `updateStudentDetails` now clears `withdrawn_at`/`graduated_at` correctly on every status transition (not just active→X) and self-heals a cancelled invoice on reactivation. Added a dedicated `reactivateStudent` action (requires currently-`withdrawn` + a class assigned) wired to a real "Reactivate" button on `StudentSettingsTab.tsx`'s Danger Zone column, so there's now a purpose-built control, not just the generic dropdown. On checking the withdraw-modal copy: it turned out accurate, not stale — `StudentSettingsTab.tsx` already has a working status dropdown (Edit Details) in the same Settings tab that can move a student back to Active; the real bug was that path silently doing the wrong thing under the hood, now fixed. No copy change needed.
- [x] **MEDIUM — `cancelInvoice()` has no closed-cycle or successor-exists guard, unlike the equivalent (and more conservative) check already implemented in `requestDiscount()`** — confirmed live: a closed-term invoice whose unpaid balance had already carried forward into a later term's invoice (via `previous_balance_from_invoice_id`) was cancelled outright with no error and no successor check. The debt itself isn't escaped — `computeInvoice.ts`'s prior-invoice lookup has no status filter, so the successor invoice keeps actively demanding that same amount — but the audit trail becomes self-contradictory (one invoice reads "cancelled, ₦50,000, unpaid" while another is actively collecting that exact ₦50,000), risking a staff member wrongly assuming the debt was written off. Align `cancelInvoice()` with `requestDiscount()`'s existing closed-cycle/successor guard. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `cancelInvoice` (`invoices/[id]/actions.ts`) now mirrors `requestDiscount`'s exact pattern — if the invoice's cycle is closed and a successor invoice exists via `previous_balance_from_invoice_id`, cancellation is blocked with an error naming the successor's cycle.
- [x] **HIGH — Sendchamp webhook's phone-number fallback matching is completely non-functional, and would be unscoped across schools even if fixed naively** — when a delivery-status webhook's `reference` doesn't exactly match `message_logs.provider_message_id`, the fallback query (match by `recipient_phone` + `status='sent'`, ordered by recency) throws a PostgREST 400 (`42703 column message_logs.created_at does not exist`) on its exact `.order()+.limit()+.select()` combination. The route swallows the error and still returns `200 {received:true}`, so the status update silently never happens. Separately confirmed `.limit()` isn't enforced on the UPDATE at all in this setup (removing the colliding clauses would update *every* matching row), and the query has no `school_id` filter, so a naive fix risks updating another school's message row for a shared phone number. Real-world impact: any delivery report whose reference doesn't cleanly match is silently lost, with no error surfaced anywhere. Fix: correct the column reference, scope by `school_id`, and cap to exactly one row (e.g. via a subquery/`id IN (SELECT ...)` pattern rather than `.limit()` on the update itself). **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `created_at` is a real column (used the same way in `reminders.ts`) — the 42703 was PostgREST failing to resolve it specifically in the `order()+limit()` combination applied to an UPDATE, not a missing column. Rewrote the fallback as a plain `SELECT ... order().limit(1)` (always valid — this is the everyday supported shape) to find the single candidate row's id, then a separate `UPDATE ... .eq('id', candidate.id)`. Updating by primary key makes the row-count guard real: at most one row can ever be touched, closing the "`.limit()` isn't enforced" hole outright. `school_id` scoping turned out not to be addable as literally worded — the webhook payload carries no school identifier at all (Sendchamp's callback URL is one shared endpoint across every school on the platform), so there's no value to filter by. Capping to exactly one row is the actual mitigation available: previously an unscoped match could silently update every school's matching row, now at most one row (the single most-recent match) is ever touched — a coincidence match against the wrong school's message to a shared phone number is still theoretically possible (documented in the code comment) but the blast radius is now bounded to one row instead of unbounded across all schools.
- [x] **`/api/dev/simulate-payment` throws an unhandled 500 with a raw Postgres constraint error on non-numeric `amount`** — no input validation before use. No data corruption occurs, and the route is dev-only/`NODE_ENV`-gated, but worth cleaning up before the route is removed for go-live. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** rejects a missing, non-numeric, or non-positive `amount` with a clean 400 before it ever reaches `applyProviderPayment`. Bundled into the same pass as the route's auth-gate fix above.
- [x] **CRITICAL — double-spend race condition in `applyProviderPayment`, reachable through the real signature-verified webhook endpoint** — confirmed live via two concurrently-fired, validly-HMAC-signed Paystack webhooks carrying two *different* legitimate references, ₦50,000 each, against a single ₦50,000 invoice. Both were accepted; final state: `paid_amount=100000` on a `total_amount=50000` invoice, `outstanding_amount=-50000`, and the ₦50,000 overflow was **not** routed to `credit_balance` — it's simply gone from any tracked balance. No row locking / `SELECT FOR UPDATE` guards the read-then-write in `src/lib/payments/applyPayment.ts`; the per-delivery idempotency guard only blocks replay of the *identical* reference, not two distinct legitimate references landing concurrently. Reproduced identically via the dev `/api/dev/simulate-payment` route. Highest-severity finding of the entire stress-test — this is real, unaccounted-for money on the production payment path, not a dev-only issue. Fix: serialize invoice-payment application per invoice (row lock or atomic RPC with re-read-under-lock), and always route any excess into `credit_balance` rather than letting it vanish into a negative `outstanding_amount`. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** added `apply_payment_to_invoice` (`db/atomic_payment_application.sql`, run once in the Supabase SQL editor) — a `plpgsql` function that takes a row lock (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) on the invoice before computing how much of the incoming payment it can actually still absorb, inserts the payment for at most that amount, then re-reads the row under the same lock so the caller gets back exactly how much was applied. `applyPayment.ts`'s per-invoice loop now calls this RPC instead of doing its own read-then-write, so two concurrent deliveries for the same invoice serialize on the lock instead of both reading the same stale `outstanding_amount` — the second delivery's RPC call simply sees the already-updated `paid_amount` and applies only what's genuinely still owed (zero, if the first delivery already covered it). Any amount left over after every open invoice is satisfied is routed to `credit_balance` via `insert_credit_balance_payment` (existing atomic RPC, `db/add_atomic_invoice_credit_functions.sql`) instead of vanishing into a negative `outstanding_amount`. Requires `db/atomic_payment_application.sql` to be run in Supabase before this is live — flagging per the standing rule that new SQL files need manual execution.
- [x] **MEDIUM — `approveDiscount` performs a non-atomic check-then-act on `status='pending'`, allowing concurrent double-approval** — confirmed live: two concurrent calls to approve the same pending discount both returned success, and the audit log recorded two separate `discount.approved` entries 2 seconds apart — genuine double-execution, not an idempotent no-op. Root cause: the pending-status check happens in a `SELECT`, but the subsequent `UPDATE ... SET status='applied'` has no `.eq('status','pending')` guard. In this run the student's `credit_balance` was 0 so no dollar-value corruption resulted, but the pattern plausibly risks double credit-balance application when balance is non-zero (not yet empirically proven). Same underlying "double-approval" symptom already flagged from the UI side (missing confirm dialog, logged above) — this confirms the risk goes deeper than UI friction, into the server action itself. Fix: add `.eq('status', 'pending')` to the UPDATE and treat a zero-row update as "already resolved." **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `approveDiscount` (`discounts/actions.ts`) now claims the request with `.update({status:'applied',...}).eq('id', discountId).eq('status', 'pending')` before doing anything else — Postgres re-checks that `WHERE` against the committed row, so a losing concurrent call gets zero rows updated back and the action returns "This request has already been resolved" instead of double-applying. The invoice recompute that follows only runs after a request is genuinely claimed.
- [x] **HIGH — approval-vs-payment race lets an invoice end up both discounted and overpaid, with the overage untracked** — confirmed live via concurrent (`Promise.all`) firing of a discount-approval click and a signed full-payment webhook against the same invoice. The approval's stale read of `paid_amount` (captured before the webhook's write landed) let a 20% discount apply 1.2s *after* the invoice was already fully paid. Final state: `total_amount=40000`, `paid_amount=50000`, `outstanding_amount=-10000`, and the ₦10,000 overage exists nowhere (`credit_balance` stayed 0). Separately, `invoices.status` stayed `pending` despite `fully_paid_at` being set — an additional status/field inconsistency from the same race.
