Same root-cause class as the double-spend race above (stale read, no lock) — the sequential-order guard (reject discount approval once `paid_amount > 0`) works correctly, but only when the two actions don't overlap in time. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `approveDiscount`'s final invoice write now carries an optimistic-concurrency guard — `.update({...}).eq('id', invoice.id).eq('paid_amount', invoice.paid_amount)` — so if a payment lands on the invoice between the approval's read and its write, `paid_amount` will have moved and the predicate matches zero rows instead of silently overwriting a now-stale computation. On that conflict, the action rolls back the credit-balance delta and discount-status claim it had already made and returns an explicit "a payment was just recorded, please try approving again" error rather than leaving a half-applied discount on an already-fully-paid invoice. Same root cause as the double-spend fix above (stale read, no lock) — here the guard is a conditional `UPDATE` rather than a row-locking RPC, since the two writers (discount approval vs. payment webhook) touch the invoice through different code paths that don't share a natural single entry point to lock inside.
- [x] **MEDIUM — unlimited manual discount stacking on one invoice, no cumulative-percentage warning or count limit** — confirmed live: three separate discounts (40% + 40% + 50% nominal, requested and approved individually) were all accepted on the same invoice with no block at request or approval time. The combined-discount cap math itself is correct and money-safe (`Math.min(subtotal, sum)`, floored at 0 — final `total_amount` was exactly 0, never negative), and the full audit trail of all three approvals was preserved. But nothing stops several individually-plausible approvals from silently zeroing out a bill in aggregate — worth a soft warning or a policy cap on cumulative discount percentage/count per invoice. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** picked the "soft warning" half of the choice, deliberately not a hard cap — a genuine full-ride scholarship should still be approvable, and a count/percentage cap would just push a legitimate case into an awkward workaround. `RequestDiscountModal.tsx` now computes `existingDiscountAmount + thisRequest` against `subtotal` live as the requester types and shows an inline amber banner once the projected cumulative crosses 50% of subtotal — `getPendingDiscountRequests` (`lib/queries/discountRequests.ts`) now also selects the invoice's `subtotal`/`discount_amount` so `DiscountRequestsList.tsx` can show the same warning as a badge on each pending request row (since the approver may be a different person than the requester, who needs their own heads-up) and again in the Approve confirm dialog's message when that request would cross the threshold. All three surfaces are informational only — nothing is blocked, matching this codebase's existing "inform, don't block" pattern for locked/risky-but-legitimate actions.
- [x] **HIGH — no validation ties a new student's `family_id` to any real relationship — pure exact-string phone match, silently reused** — confirmed live and *unintentionally reproduced* during this same test run: creating an unrelated test student using the standing test contact phone silently merged it into a pre-existing family record created by a completely different concurrent test agent (confirmed by the family's stored parent name staying as the other agent's test name, not what was entered). Same underlying gap already logged above from the students-domain agent (`addStudent()` family-matching has no secondary identity check), now additionally confirmed as a direct sibling-discount abuse vector: the moment a school configures non-zero sibling-discount tiers (most will), anyone entering an existing family's phone number at enrollment — by mistake or on purpose — silently qualifies for an unearned sibling discount with zero confirmation step anywhere in the single-add or CSV-bulk-import path. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** same fix as the duplicate finding above — see that entry for the full writeup (phone normalization on both paths, staff confirmation dialog on `addStudent()`, `failedRows` flagging on CSV import).
- [x] **`/discounts` "Approve" has no confirmation dialog, unlike Reject and Revoke** — clicking Approve applies the discount and recomputes the invoice immediately with no confirm step and no undo path; every sibling action on the same page (Reject, "Cancel invoice," both Revoke flows) shows a confirm dialog first. A single mis-click permanently applies a discount. Reproduced twice independently (once via an accidental double-click loop). Fix: add the same `ConfirmDialog` used elsewhere on this page to the Approve action. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `DiscountRequestsList.tsx`'s Approve button now opens the same `ConfirmDialog` component used elsewhere on the page ("Approve this discount? This will recompute the invoice immediately.") before calling `approveDiscount`, matching Reject/Revoke.
- [ ] **Invoice detail page doesn't refresh after "Cancel invoice" completes** — the server action succeeds immediately (DB confirmed cancelled within ~1s), but the page keeps showing the old "pending" badge and active "Cancel invoice"/"Request discount" buttons for over a second with no re-render; only a manual reload shows the correct cancelled state (badge, "Cancelled — no payment due," actions collapsed to View/Print). Likely a missing `router.refresh()`/revalidation call after the cancel action. Could let a user click actions against a state that's already gone stale. **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-16, left open:** `handleCancelInvoice` in `InvoiceDetailLayout.tsx` already calls `router.refresh()` immediately after a successful `cancelInvoice()` — no missing call found by code review, and neither the page nor `getInvoiceByIdForSchool` opts into any fetch/route caching that would explain a stuck stale render (Next 16 defaults fetch to `no-store`, and the page's use of `cookies()` already forces dynamic rendering). Could not reproduce live this session. Left open for re-verification during the full browser walkthrough — either the observed staleness was ordinary refresh latency (network round-trip for the refetch, not a true stuck cache) misread as permanent, or a real client-side cause not yet found (e.g. a stale prop/state timing issue only visible under real interaction). Not marking fixed without seeing it resolved live.
- [x] **`/fees` overview and cycle-detail "Outstanding" totals wrongly include a cancelled invoice's carry-forward balance (dashboard is correct, the other two aren't)** — confirmed live: a cancelled invoice carrying a "prev. balance carry-forward" line (₦100,000 total: ₦50k tuition + ₦50k carry-forward) is correctly excluded from the Dashboard Outstanding figure (₦536,000, matches a manual sum of all non-cancelled invoices) but still counted on `/fees` and cycle-detail (both ₦636,000 — off by exactly that invoice's total). Only cancelled invoices carrying a carry-forward line leak; plain cancelled invoices don't. Root cause likely: the carry-forward balance is aggregated on a path that isn't gated on invoice `status != 'cancelled'`, unlike the main outstanding sum. Cross-page inconsistency between three surfaces showing the same conceptual number. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** the suspected root cause (an ungated carry-forward sum) didn't hold up — `getDashboardKPIs`, `getFeesOverview`, and `getCycleDetailById` (`lib/queries/dashboard.ts`, `lib/queries/fees.ts`) all already filter to `status !== 'cancelled'` identically before summing, and `total_amount` already embeds the carry-forward line, so excluding the row excludes the whole amount on every path. The real cause was missing cache invalidation: `cancelInvoice` (`invoices/[id]/actions.ts`) only called `revalidatePath` for `/invoices/[id]`, `/invoices`, and the student page — never for `/fees`, `/fees/cycles`, or the specific `/fees/cycles/[id]` cycle-detail page, unlike every sibling invoice-mutating action (`regenerateInvoice`, `closeTermAndCarryForward`) which do revalidate those routes. `/fees` and cycle-detail kept serving their pre-cancellation cached render (still counting the cancelled invoice) until something else happened to bust that cache; the Dashboard only looked "correct" because it hadn't been cached from before the cancellation in the same session — not because its query logic differed. Added `billing_cycle_id` to `cancelInvoice`'s invoice select and three missing calls (`revalidatePath('/fees')`, `revalidatePath('/fees/cycles')`, `revalidatePath(`/fees/cycles/${invoice.billing_cycle_id}`)`) alongside the existing ones.
- [x] **Cancelled invoice's own PAYMENT summary card still shows a stale non-zero "Outstanding" figure** — even though the ACTIONS panel correctly says "Cancelled — nothing to send" and PAYMENT INSTRUCTIONS correctly says "Cancelled — no payment due," the top PAYMENT card still shows e.g. "Outstanding ₦59,500" in the same orange styling used for genuinely unpaid invoices. Confirmed on two independent cancelled invoices (systemic, not a one-off). Reads as money still owed on a voided invoice. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** the PAYMENT card's Outstanding row now branches on `status === 'cancelled'` first and shows a plain gray "Cancelled" label instead of a dollar figure, matching the ACTIONS/PAYMENT INSTRUCTIONS panels it was inconsistent with.
- [ ] **"Regenerate all" doesn't refresh the cycle-detail page's own data after the job completes** — toast says "regeneration complete — 2/3, 1 failed" and a banner says "2 invoices updated," but the "N invoices are out of date" alert, KPI cards, and every row's TOTAL/OUTSTANDING/status stay frozen at pre-job values with no client refetch; only a manual reload shows the true post-job state. Same missing-refresh pattern as the cancel-invoice bug found in the discounts domain testing (see above) — likely a shared root cause worth fixing once across both actions. **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-16, left open:** `runRegenerateAllJob`'s completion callback in `CycleDetailLayout.tsx` already calls `router.refresh()` on both the normal-completion and cancelled branches (present in the last commit before this stress-test pass, not missing) — the same shared root cause the note above suspected, but it's already wired on this side too. `invoices`/`totalsByStatus` are read straight from the `data` prop with no local state shadowing it, so a fresh server render would flow through correctly. No missing call or stale-state bug found by code review. Left open alongside the cancel-invoice case above for the same reason — re-verify live during the full browser walkthrough rather than mark fixed on code review alone.
- [x] **"N invoices are out of date" banner count doesn't match what "Regenerate all" actually processes, with no explanation surfaced to the user** — banner said "6 out of date"; the actual job only targeted 3 (network-confirmed `total:3`), because 3 of the 6 are "Locked" (paid + out of date, correctly excluded to protect paid invoices) and silently skipped by the bulk action with no count breakdown shown anywhere in the UI. Of the 3 it did target, 1 failed server-side ("Student is withdrawn, not active") with the error never surfaced in the UI (only visible via network inspection) — only 2 of the original 6 actually got resolved, and the admin has no way to know why the rest are still flagged. Fix: show a breakdown ("3 of 6 can be auto-regenerated; 3 are locked against paid invoices") and surface per-item failures from the job result, not just an aggregate toast. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** the "out of date" banner on `CycleDetailLayout` now appends a breakdown line ("N can be auto-regenerated; N is/are locked against payments already made") computed from the same `needsRegeneration`/`regenerationBlocked` fields already on each invoice row. The post-job completion toast now also lists distinct failure reasons pulled from the job result's `failures` array (e.g. "1 failed (Student is withdrawn, not active)") instead of only reporting a bare processed count — this applies to both the normal-completion and cancelled-mid-job toast branches.
- [x] **Discount revoke UI is wired only to a student's current-billing-cycle invoice — an approved, unpaid, unsent discount on an older (closed-cycle) invoice is unreachable from anywhere in the app** — confirmed by direct code trace: `getStudentById` (`src/lib/queries/students.ts:230-254`) resolves `currentInvoice` strictly as the invoice belonging to whichever cycle has `status='active'`, with no fallback; `discounts`/`canFullyRevokeDiscount` (lines 298-339) are computed only when `currentInvoice` exists and are scoped `.eq('invoice_id', currentInvoice.id)`. The student page (`students/[id]/page.tsx:111-116`) passes exactly that into `ApplyDiscountButton`, the only component in the codebase that calls `revokeDiscount`. The invoice detail page (`InvoiceDetailLayout.tsx`/`discountActions.ts`) only ever implements `requestDiscount` — no revoke action or UI exists there at all. Net effect, reproduced live: a student whose active-term invoice hasn't been generated yet (e.g. new term's fee items not set up) shows "Apply discount" fully disabled ("Generate this term's invoice first"), even though an older term's invoice has a fully-revocable discount sitting on it — there is no path to revoke it. Fix: either have the invoice detail page's own discount panel support revoke directly on whichever invoice is being viewed, or have the student-header button fall back to the most recent invoice with a revocable discount when there's no current-cycle invoice. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** took the second fix direction — `getStudentById` now falls back to the most recent invoice with a one-off (non-recurring) applied discount whenever the current-cycle invoice has none of its own, exposing `fallbackDiscounts`/`fallbackCanFullyRevoke`/`fallbackDiscountInvoiceId`. `ApplyDiscountButton` uses that fallback whenever `currentInvoice` has nothing, so revoke is reachable again — while "add a new discount" correctly stays gated on an actual current-cycle invoice existing (there's nothing to attach a new request to otherwise). A recurring discount was deliberately left out of the fallback: it keeps reapplying every term once invoices generate, so it was never actually stuck the way a one-off discount from a closed term could be.
- [x] **LOW/INFORMATIONAL — no upper-bound sanity cap or anomaly alerting on webhook-reported payment amounts** — an absurd amount (₦9,999,999,999) on a valid signed webhook is accepted in full and routed to `credit_balance` with no flag; a negative webhook amount is silently absorbed as a no-op (accepted `200`, no state change) rather than rejected or logged as suspicious. Architecturally by-design under the DVA trust model (the provider is trusted once signature-verified), but worth defense-in-depth alerting on outlier amounts given the double-spend/race findings above already show real money can end up untracked. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `applyProviderPayment` now rejects any non-positive `amountPaid` outright (`throw`) instead of falling through to an empty candidate loop and a silent no-op — the webhook processors already wrap this call in a `try/catch` that records the failure on the webhook-event row (`status: 'error'`, with the message) for manual follow-up, so a negative/zero amount now surfaces instead of vanishing as an unremarked `200`. Separately, amounts at or above a ₦5,000,000 threshold (`SUSPICIOUS_PAYMENT_THRESHOLD`) are still applied in full — deliberately not blocked, since a school can legitimately collect a full year's fees in one transfer — but now also fire a best-effort `admin_notifications` insert flagging the payment for a human to glance at, so an outlier is visible rather than silent. Not a hard cap (none was warranted architecturally, per the finding's own framing) but the "no alerting at all" gap is closed.

**🔴 Critical incident during this stress-test (self-inflicted, not a product bug) — full detail:** one adversarial test agent, while cleaning up its own staff-invite test fixture, called the Supabase Admin API's `GET /auth/v1/admin/users?email=<address>` endpoint under the incorrect assumption that it filters by the `email` query param — **it does not**; it silently returns an unfiltered, paginated list of every auth user in the project. The agent's cleanup script then looped over that full list and issued a `DELETE` for every ID returned. **8 deletes were issued, 7 succeeded and are permanently unrecoverable** (the 8th was the agent's own intended target). Because `public.users.id` has `ON DELETE CASCADE` back to `auth.users(id)`, each of the 7 also cascade-deleted that user's application profile row with no recovery path — these almost certainly belonged to other concurrently-running test agents' fixtures. Two of the 8 delete calls returned HTTP 500 and failed — by chance, those two IDs were the real school-owner accounts (`paystack@school.com`, `monnify@school.com`), both confirmed intact immediately afterward and again in final verification. The incident was self-disclosed the moment it was discovered; the agent switched to an exact-ID-only rule for every subsequent admin/DB call for the rest of the engagement, recreated its own lost test account, and ran a final audit confirming exactly the 2 real owner accounts remain system-wide with no stray test fixtures left behind. **Takeaway for future test runs: never call Supabase's admin users-list endpoint with a query-param filter and trust it — list-then-filter client-side, or use exact IDs only.**

**🆕 New security findings from adversarial testing (2026-09-16) — see "Security & go-live hardening" below for tracking:**
- [x] No login rate limiting (brute-force risk) — confirmed live: 15 rapid consecutive wrong-password attempts against a known account all returned identical fast responses with no throttling, delay, or lockout — tracked below. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** see the full writeup under "Security & go-live hardening" below — per-account lockout (5 failed attempts / 15 min) backed by a new `login_attempts` table.
- [x] **CRITICAL** — `/api/dev/simulate-payment` and `/api/dev/provision-dva` perform real financial-state mutations (simulate a payment, provision a virtual account for any student) and are gated only by a `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` check in application code — no session/role auth at all. Any staging, preview, or misconfigured deployment exposes both to unauthenticated callers — tracked below. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** both routes now also require a real authenticated staff session via `requirePermission` (`manage-invoices` for simulate-payment, `manage-payment-config` for provision-dva — matching the permission gates on their real, non-dev equivalents) on top of the existing `NODE_ENV` check, and both now scope the target student lookup to the caller's own `school_id` (previously any authorized caller could pass another school's `studentId`). `simulate-payment` also picked up the input-validation fix from the sibling finding below (rejects non-numeric/non-positive `amount` with a clean 400 instead of a raw Postgres error). Both routes are still dev-only and still slated for outright removal before go-live (tracked below) — this is defense in depth for the interval until then, not a reason to skip that removal.
- [x] Missing permission check on a PDF-generation route — confirmed as `/api/cycles/[id]/pdf`: a staff account explicitly configured with zero permissions can still download a school's full cycle invoice PDF bundle (auth is checked, granular permission is not) — tracked below. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** added `requirePermission('see-invoices')` at the top of the route, matching the gate already on the single-invoice PDF route (`/api/invoices/[id]/pdf`) it was inconsistent with.
- [x] **New** — dead `/signup` route contains a live privilege-escalation path (`signup()` server action inserts `role: 'super_admin', school_id: null` on success) — currently unreachable (blocked unconditionally by `middleware.ts` for all HTTP methods on that path prefix, never compiled/referenced elsewhere), so not exploitable today, but a foot-gun if the middleware rule is ever loosened or refactored. Recommend deleting the route/action outright rather than relying on middleware as the only defense — tracked below. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** deleted `src/app/signup/` outright (`page.tsx` + `actions.ts`) rather than continuing to rely on the middleware block as the only defense, per the recommendation above. `middleware.ts`'s `/signup` references are now inert (no route left to protect) and left in place — harmless, and removing them isn't necessary for the fix.

**🆕 New gaps found during the final phase of the full onboarding-through-year-end Playwright walkthrough (2026-09-16, real browser UI against "Greenfield Comprehensive Academy," `/fees/year-end` full wizard run — see `pw-test-fees101/walkthrough/FINDINGS.md`'s "Year-end rollover" section for the full narrative):**
- [x] **CRITICAL — "Repeat class" promotion override silently promotes the student instead of keeping them in their current class.** `YearEndRolloverWizard.tsx`'s per-row `targetClassId` state initializes to the suggested *promoted* class id (default action is "Promote"), and the action `<select>`'s `onChange` only reset `targetClassId` for the `'promote'` branch — switching to `'repeat'` left that stale promoted-class id in state, which `buildDecisionList()` sent through unconditionally and `startYearEndRollover`'s `promotionRows` mapping stored as `to_class_id`, which the apply step's `else if (promo.to_class_id)` guard then applied as a real class change regardless of `action`. Confirmed live: a student explicitly set to "Repeat class" in Primary 1 ended up promoted to Primary 2 by a real rollover run. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** fixed at three layers — (1) the row `<select>`'s `onChange` now resets `targetClassId` to `row.currentClassId` for `'repeat'` (and keeps `row.suggestedTargetClassId` for `'promote'`); (2) `buildDecisionList()` only includes `targetClassId` in the payload for `action === 'promote'`; (3) `startYearEndRollover`'s server-side `promotionRows` mapping (`fees/cycles/actions.ts`) only sets `to_class_id` for `action === 'promote'`, so `'repeat'` is guaranteed `to_class_id: null` independent of client state. The one live student incorrectly moved by this bug during testing was corrected back via the real Edit-student UI, not a DB write.
- [x] **Closed billing cycles show a stale invoice-count denominator once the school's active-student count later changes (e.g. after a graduation).** `getAllCycles()`/`getCycleDetailById()` (`src/lib/queries/fees.ts`) compute `totalActiveStudents` as a single live, school-wide count and apply it uniformly to every cycle row including closed/historical ones, whose actual student count at the time can no longer match the present roster. Confirmed live: graduating a student via year-end rollover (5→4 active students) made two already-closed historical terms both start showing "5 / 4" on `/fees/cycles`. Pre-existing latent bug, never triggered before this walkthrough's first graduation event. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `CyclesLayout.tsx`'s detail KPI tile and term-list row now suppress the "/ totalActiveStudents" denominator entirely for `status === 'closed'` cycles, showing the plain invoice count instead. `totalActiveStudents` itself and the underlying live-count query were left unchanged — correct as-is for the current active cycle, and adding a historical roster snapshot would be a schema change out of scope for a display bug.
- [x] The "Manual 'Roll forward fees from' does not carry forward student-level fee opt-ins, but year-end rollover does" item further above — **its remaining open question is now closed via live re-verification in this final phase.** Year-end rollover was driven for real end-to-end and both fee items (recurring ones, via the shared `createTerm(..., rollForwardFromCycleId)` call) and student-level fee adjustments/opt-ins (via the shared `carryForwardFeeAdjustments` call) were confirmed correctly present on the new term without any manual re-creation — no divergence from manual roll-forward found. See `FINDINGS.md` for the live checks performed (fee items per class, School Bus opt-in state).

**🆕 New gaps found during post-fix verification pass (2026-09-16, background agents re-checking the shipped work above against live data):**
- [x] **Manually closing a term wrote two audit-log rows for one action.** `closeTerm` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts:754-785`) calls `closeTermAndCarryForward`, which already logs `term.closed_carried_forward` (line 525) with the full close/carry-forward summary as metadata — `closeTerm` then logged a second, redundant `term.closed` event for the same term id right after, confirmed live via 5 separate historical incidents in the `audit_log` table (paired rows ~0.15-3.4s apart) plus a walkthrough screenshot (`pw-test-fees101/walkthrough/88-audit-log.png`) showing both "Term closed" and "Term closed & carried forward" for the same term. Not a double-submit or React double-invoke — one click, one server-action call, two `logAuditEvent` writes. `activateTerm` and the year-end rollover adopt-path both call the same shared helper correctly (no duplicate) since they either log a distinct event for a *different* entity or don't log a follow-up at all. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** removed the redundant `logAuditEvent` block from `closeTerm`; `closeTermAndCarryForward`'s own `term.closed_carried_forward` event remains the single record for a manual close (it already covers "closed with nothing to carry forward" via its own summary branching). `term.closed` is no longer emitted anywhere — its `ACTION_LABELS` entry (`auditLogLabels.ts`) was left in place as harmless unused mapping rather than removed.
- [x] **A leading/trailing space in a student's name broke their avatar initials while the name heading still rendered fine — confirmed live 2026-09-16.** `addStudent()` (`students/actions.ts:112-113`) and `updateStudentDetails()` (`students/[id]/actions.ts:126-127`) inserted `first_name`/`last_name` raw with no `.trim()`, inconsistent with the same files' own `primaryParentName.trim()` handling. A leading space (realistic paste/typo) made `getInitials()` (`students/[id]/page.tsx:28`, `(firstName[0] + lastName[0])`) read the space as the first character — browsers collapse leading whitespace in running text, so the full name heading still displayed correctly while the avatar circle silently dropped to one visible letter. CSV import was already unaffected (`import/actions.ts:111`'s `getValue()` already trims every cell). **FIXED 2026-09-16:** added `.trim()` to both insert/update sites, and hardened `getInitials()` itself to trim and guard empty strings (`(f + l).toUpperCase() || '?'`) as defense in depth. Checked the three other initials helpers in the codebase (`UserMenu.tsx`, `SchoolProfileForm.tsx`, `InvoicePDF.tsx`) — all three already split on `/\s+/` with `.filter(Boolean)`, which is immune to this exact bug; no change needed there.
- [x] **Invoice detail page showed a carried-forward previous-term balance twice, under two different labels — confirmed live 2026-09-16, presentation-only (no double-count).** `InvoiceDetailLayout.tsx`'s line-items table rendered the carry-forward as a tagged row ("Outstanding balance from [Term] (carry-forward)", line ~265), and the footer summary (added by the "gross + credit" ticket) rendered the *same* amount again as "Previous balance carried forward" (line ~296) — same invoice, same ₦X, two labels, easy to misread as double-billing when naively summing every visible row. Verified via `computeInvoice.ts` that `total`/`outstanding_amount` add `previousBalance` exactly once (`subtotal` deliberately excludes it) — the underlying money was always correct. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** removed the carry-forward row from the line-items table body (filtered `kind !== 'previous_balance'` before mapping) — the footer's existing "Previous balance carried forward" row is the one place it's now shown, which is also the row that mathematically bridges Subtotal → Total, so nothing needed re-explaining there. Left `InvoicePDF.tsx` untouched — it has no footer breakdown, so its line-item mention is the *only* place the carry-forward is explained on that document and must stay.
- [x] **Active terms could still have their dates/name edited after going live — fixed 2026-09-21.** `updateTerm` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts`) had a closed-term guard but nothing stopping an admin from renaming or reshuffling the dates of a term that was already billing, sending invoices, and taking payments — a change there could silently desync what parents were already told from what the system now says. Added a guard returning `{ error: 'This term is live, so its dates and name are locked. Undo its activation first (only possible before anything has been sent or paid) if you need to change them.' }` for `status === 'active'`; the three `reopenTermAsDraft` lock-reason messages (any paid / any sent / any carried-in) were reworded to match (no more pointing at a since-removed "Edit term details" affordance). UI: the "Edit term details" action no longer renders for an active term in `CyclesLayout.tsx`/`CycleDetailLayout.tsx` — only "Undo activation" and "Review close term" remain while live.
- [x] **Cycles/CycleDetail/FeeStructure UI cleanup batch — shipped 2026-09-21, live-screenshot-verified.** Bundle of Modernist-redesign consistency fixes across the term lifecycle surfaces: removed the redundant "What this term did" recap section from closed-term views (the numbers it repeated are already on screen above); confirmed cycle-detail's invoice pagination is genuinely server-driven (`getCycleDetailById` re-fetches per `router.push`) and fixed the footer count mismatch — the always-unpaginated "students without an invoice" list was rendering on every page while the footer only counted paginated invoices, so it now only renders on the invoice table's last page and the footer counts both lists explicitly; added a `from=` back-link from "Edit fees" (Fee Structure page) back to the term it was opened from, instead of that page just replacing whatever was on screen; converted the remaining bordered outline buttons on these three surfaces to plain text actions, matching the rest of the redesign (bordered buttons only remain for the one primary CTA per surface, e.g. "Activate term"); removed the `invoiceCount === 0` gate on "Delete draft" in both `CyclesLayout.tsx` and `CycleDetailLayout.tsx` — `deleteTermDraft` already cascades fees/invoices/adjustments correctly and only blocks on an actually-sent invoice, the gate was just hiding a working action; "Print all" now also shows on draft terms once invoices exist, not just closed ones; the Cycles page's selected-term heading is now also a clickable link to the term overview (list-row titles already were); removed the standalone "Year-end rollover" button from the Cycles page header (redundant with the dedicated Year-end tab on the main Fees page); `CreateTermPanel.tsx` rebuilt from a sticky inline card into the same right-edge slide-over overlay used by `FeeFormPanel.tsx` (dimmed backdrop, 420px `<aside>`, single scrolling column); fixed an em-dash in `FeeStructureLayout.tsx`'s coverage-gap insight text (house style is no em-dashes). See the item above for the related active-term edit lock shipped alongside this batch.
- [x] **Cycles follow-up batch — shipped 2026-09-21, live-screenshot-verified against `App Shell.dc.html`.** Four fixes from live owner testing of the batch above. **[Corrected] Step action buttons:** the batch above converted every numbered lifecycle-step action (draft's "Edit dates"/"Edit fees"/"Draft invoices", active's "Review close") to plain text, on the theory that this matched "the rest of the redesign" — re-checked against the canvas's `cycleSteps` render template (`App Shell.dc.html` lines ~890-920, `<button class="{{ s.btn }}">` where `s.btn` is `'bo'`/`'bi'`), which shows the numbered-step row template always uses a real bordered/filled button, never borderless text — that borderless `.txt` convention is specific to generic table rows (e.g. the Discounts queue), a different template. Reverted: `CyclesLayout.tsx`'s step actions (draft, active, and the new closed-term steps below) now render as `m-btn-outline`/`m-btn-primary`, matching "Activate term." **Closed-term quick-info panel:** closed terms showed only a bare paragraph + Print all/View term overview, unlike draft/active's step recap. Added a read-only 3-4 step recap (same `Step` shape/`renderStep` now shared at module scope) — invoices generated, invoices sent, final collection %, and (conditionally) an outstanding-balance step when a closed term still has money owed. **Back-nav bug:** `CyclesLayout.tsx`'s two "Edit fees" links passed `from=/fees/cycles` with no cycle id, so `FeeStructureLayout.tsx`'s back link always landed on whichever term is active/first rather than the term that was actually selected; fixed to `from=/fees/cycles?cycle={id}` (matching `CycleDetailLayout.tsx`'s already-correct pattern). **`CreateTermPanel.tsx` Session/Activate-immediately restyle:** the session radio group and "Activate immediately" checkbox were plain `<input>` markup, inconsistent with "Add a fee"'s bordered checkbox-card look; rebuilt using the same `Mark`/`SectionLabel`/`OptRow` pattern `FeeFormPanel.tsx`/`EditFeeGroupPanel.tsx` each already duplicate, with one addition (`OptRow` gained `children` support so the session `<select>` and new-session date fields can render inline under the selected row). tsc clean; all four fixes CDP-screenshot-verified live.
- [x] **Cycle-detail page's per-row Total reportedly disagreeing with the Students page's Total-Expected on the Scale Test School — investigated 2026-09-16, not a bug.** Confirmed the `getCycleDetailById` server-side pagination change (`943fc89`) is not implicated: every KPI/aggregate (`totalsByStatus`, `totalExpected`, `totalOutstanding`, `lockedOutOfDate`, `autoRegenerableCount`) is computed from the full unpaginated invoice list before the filter/pagination slice runs (`fees.ts:766-806`), and a row's `totalAmount` mapping (`fees.ts:660`) is untouched by that diff. The actual explanation: the cycle-detail row shows the invoice's **stored** `total_amount` (frozen at generation time), while a student's own Fees tab shows a **live recompute** (`expectedBill`, via `computeInvoiceForStudent`) — these intentionally diverge whenever a fee item changes after an invoice was generated, until "Update invoice"/"Regenerate all" is run, exactly matching the existing "N invoices are out of date" mechanism (`fees.ts:694-735`, pre-dates the pagination commit). The Students-list "Total expected" column reads the same stored field as cycle-detail for the same cycle, so those two never disagree — the real comparison being made was against the recomputed figure on the student's own page. No code change needed; recommendation is to check the cycle-detail "out of date" filter tab before treating a Total mismatch as a defect.
- [x] **Year-end rollover discoverability nudges — shipped 2026-09-22.** Year-End Rollover (`/fees/year-end`, `src/lib/yearEnd/promotion`) is the only place that ever promotes a student to their next class or graduates an exit-year student. Two other paths let a school fully switch academic years without ever running it: Academic structure's "Current session" drawer (`SessionsTab.tsx` → `createSession`/`setActiveSession`) and Billing Cycles' "Create term" panel (`CreateTermPanel.tsx` → `createTerm`) under a draft session — confirmed by reading `setActiveSession` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts`), which is a bare status flip with no promotion logic at all. Left unnoticed, every student stays parked in last year's class forever. Owner-approved three additive, informational-only nudges (this app's existing "inform, don't block" pattern — see the discount-threshold and close-term-preview items above); no existing business logic changed, Create session/Create term behave exactly as before. **(1) `SessionsTab.tsx`:** the "+ New session" form now shows a plain paragraph (no border/tint, matching this same file's existing "Invoices are filed under the session current..." note) — *"Creating a session on its own doesn't move any students up a class. If this is for a new academic year, use Year-End Rollover instead — it promotes students and can create the new session for you."* — linked to `/fees/year-end` only when the viewer has `run-year-end` (`useCan`), plain text otherwise. **(2) `CreateTermPanel.tsx`:** the same style of note now appears directly below the session picker, reusing the panel's existing `activationBlockedByDraftSession` condition (`sessionMode === 'existing' && selectedSession?.status === 'draft'` — never true for the active session or the inline "New session" path) — *"{Session name} isn't the active session yet. A term created here won't move any students up a class — when you're ready to make this the live academic year, use Year-End Rollover instead."* — same `run-year-end` link gating. **(3) `CyclesLayout.tsx`:** a new `activeSessionWrappedUp` boolean, computed client-side from the `cycles`/`sessions` props the page already fetches (no new query, no page.tsx change needed). **Exact trigger:** the currently active session (`sessions.find(s => s.status === 'active')`) has zero cycles with `status !== 'closed'` among its own billing cycles, AND at least one cycle with `status === 'closed'`. Deliberately scoped to that session's own cycles rather than reusing the page's pre-existing global `allTermsClosed` check (which can disagree — e.g. a draft term prepped under a different, not-yet-active session), and deliberately requires an existing closed cycle so a brand-new school that has never activated a first term never triggers it. When true, shows a neutral ink-bordered panel (`pl-3 py-2 border-l-2 border-[var(--color-ink)]` — same weight as the page's existing error/unmatched-adjustments panels, just ink instead of signal/ochre) — *"{Session name} has no more terms to activate. If the academic year is done, run Year-End Rollover to promote students and open the next one."* — with a "Go to Year-End Rollover" outline button shown only when `run-year-end` is present (text-only otherwise, matching the pre-existing `allTermsClosed` banner's own permission-gated-button precedent a few lines below it). Stateless by design — no dismiss/snooze/localStorage; it's a live computed state that simply stops rendering once a new term is activated under that session. **Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. **Not live-tested this session** — the persistent CDP Chrome profile was signed out at `/login` with no credentials on hand to sign back in, so this was verified by reading every touched file's exact types/conditions (`SessionRow`/`CycleRow` shapes in `lib/queries/fees.ts`, `activationBlockedByDraftSession`, `useCan`/`PermissionsProvider`) and reasoning through the specific data states (fresh school with zero closed cycles → no panel; active session with an open draft term → no panel; active session's only term closed with nothing else open → panel shows). Needs a real browser pass (all three states) before this is treated as fully confirmed.
- [x] **Cross-session term activation now BLOCKED, not just nudged — shipped 2026-09-22, owner: "activating it will or should require a rollover and closing the previous term and session."** The nudges above are informational only; this one is a real behaviour change to `activateTerm` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts:609`), the shared action behind every "Activate term" control in the app (`CyclesLayout.tsx`, `CycleDetailLayout.tsx`, and the close-term-first routing in `CloseTermLedger.tsx` — three call sites, one function). Previously, activating a draft term under a session other than the current one silently called `setActiveSession` (closing the old session) and `closeTermAndCarryForward` on the old term (carries forward the ₦ balance only) with **zero student promotion** — the exact trap the nudges above warn about, except this was the point where it actually happened, not just a place that could lead to it. New check, added right after the existing past-session guard: if a currently-active session exists and differs from the target term's session, `activateTerm` now returns an error instead of proceeding — *""{Session name}" isn't the current session — activating a term there is a new academic year, not just the next term. Use Year-End Rollover instead: it closes out the current year properly and promotes every student to their next class first."* Same-session term-to-term activation (the normal case — Term 2 → Term 3 within one academic year) is completely unaffected, and a brand-new school activating its very first term ever (no active session yet) is unaffected too — the block only fires when an active session already exists and isn't the target's own. **Confirmed this does not regress Year-End Rollover itself:** `continueYearEndRollover` (`fees/year-end/actions.ts`) never calls `activateTerm` — it has its own fully separate activation/promotion code path, so rollover remains the one legitimate way to make a new session's term live. **Confirmed interaction with the close-term-first flow (2026-09-21 entry above):** when `CloseTermLedger.handleClose` closes the old term and then calls `activateTerm` on a cross-session draft, it now hits this new block — the old term ends up closed with nothing activated in its place until Rollover runs, which is the intended outcome, not a bug. Fixed one real piece of now-misleading copy this surfaced: the close-then-activate-failed message in `CloseTermLedger.tsx` had a hardcoded trailing "You can activate it from Cycles." — false for this specific failure (retrying from Cycles fails the same way) — removed, since every `activateTerm` error message is already written to be self-explanatory about the actual next step. tsc clean. **Not live-tested** (same signed-out CDP profile as the entry above) — verified by tracing `activateTerm`'s exact control flow and all three call sites. **Flagged, not investigated further:** whether starting Year-End Rollover when the previous term is already closed (but its session is still marked active, as this new block deliberately leaves it) is itself a state Rollover already tolerates — the existing 2026-09-16 stress-test hardening suggests likely yes, but this specific sequence (close-term-first routing hitting this new block, then the owner opening Rollover afterward) hasn't been explicitly walked through.

- [x] **RLS self-escalation + deactivation lockdown** — found via impersonation stress-testing: `"Users update self"` had no `WITH CHECK`, so any signed-in user could self-promote to `school_admin`, change their own `role_id`, flip `is_active` back on, or move `school_id` via a direct API call, independent of any app-layer permission check. `is_school_owner()`/`is_super_admin()`/`current_school_id()` also didn't check `is_active`, so a deactivated owner/admin kept DB-level read+write access. Fixed via `db/rls_privilege_fixes.sql` — self-update now pins `role`/`role_id`/`is_active`/`school_id` to their stored values, and the three helper functions all gate on `is_active`. Applied + verified on the dev/test Supabase (2026-09-02). **Must also be run against production before launch** — folded into the go-live checklist below.

New shared `FieldEditDrawer.tsx` (`src/components/settings/`) — the same 420px right-edge shell as `FeeFormPanel`/`RequestDiscountModal`/`ApplyDiscountButton`'s drawer (translucent ink backdrop, 2px ink left border, header + signal-red Close, then Current-value box / field-specific New-value slot (children) / optional canvas-sourced note / Reason (optional) input / "Recorded as **{actor}** in Team & Trust → Audit log." / Save change + Cancel) — one deliberate deviation from the canvas kept: its Close button is a plain grey `.bg` label, the app's own drawer convention (already shipped in 3+ other drawers) uses uppercase signal-red Close, kept for cross-drawer consistency. `SchoolProfileForm.tsx` rewritten: `SettingRow` simplified back to a static label/value/action row with a single `onAction` callback (no more inline-edit branch), and all 7 fields — School name, Logo, Address, Office phone, SMS sender name, Email address, Proprietress/Owner name — now open `FieldEditDrawer` instead of expanding in place. **Logo** is the most detailed: a dashed drop-zone ("Drop an image here" / "Choose a file", reusing the existing `handleLogoFile` upload handler unchanged), a "How it will print" section showing two real 56×56 swatches side by side — the actual current logo (or initials) plainly, and the same image with `filter: grayscale(1) contrast(1.3) brightness(1.6)` against an ink background as a genuine preview of black-and-white print rendering, not a decorative mock — plus an inline "Remove logo" link that expands a ruled confirm panel (2px ink left-rule, Remove/Cancel) in place of the old centred `ConfirmDialog`, per the app's established never-stack-a-modal-on-a-modal rule. **Address** kept its existing 3 separate inputs (Street/City/State) rather than collapsing to the canvas's single textarea, since the DB stores them as separate columns — a deliberate scope-preserving call, not a data-model rewrite. Plumbed an optional free-text `reason` from each drawer into the audit log with no schema change (`metadata` is already JSONB): `updateSchoolGeneralInfo`/`uploadSchoolLogo`/`removeSchoolLogo` (`settings/actions.ts`) each gained an optional `reason` param folded into their existing `logAuditEvent` metadata. Added the actor's real name to the drawer's "Recorded as" line — `settings/page.tsx` now looks up `users.name` for the signed-in admin and passes it down as `actorName` (no prior client hook exposed this; the Sidebar's own display name comes from a page-local query, not a reusable one). **Bug caught during live verification, fixed:** the Proprietress drawer's Title/First/Last inputs rendered stacked in one column instead of 3-across — `className="grid grid-cols-3 gap-2"` never made it into the loaded stylesheet (confirmed via `getComputedStyle` and a direct stylesheet-rules scan: `gridTemplateColumns` was a single `374px` track, and no `.grid-cols-3` rule existed anywhere in the page's CSS) because this exact bare utility, with no responsive prefix, had never been used anywhere else in the codebase before this file — the known WASM-Tailwind gap of not always picking up a brand-new utility class on first use (see [[tailwind-dynamic-class-safelist]]). Fixed by switching to an explicit inline `style={{ display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: '80px 1fr 1fr', gap: 8 }}` (title column narrower than name columns), consistent with how heavily this file already leans on inline styles elsewhere; re-verified live, renders 3-across correctly. tsc clean; **live-verified via CDP for all 7 drawers** (School name, Logo, Address, Office phone, SMS sender name, Email address, Proprietress) — each opens correctly with the right Current value, New-value input(s) pre-filled from current data, canvas-sourced note text where the canvas defines one (School name, Logo, Address only — Office phone/SMS/Email/Proprietress have none, matching the canvas's own `fieldFor()` fallback), correct "Recorded as School Owner" attribution, and Save/Cancel (Logo: Done/Cancel). Verified non-destructively (opened, checked rendering, cancelled) rather than saving, to avoid overwriting the live test school's real profile data. **NOT committed.**
            - **[x] Logo drawer upload bug — root-caused and fixed (2026-09-22, owner: "it keeps saying uploading after i have placed the image but i should not be able to click done until its done no? and does the uplaod work becasue it just says uplaoding").** Two real bugs in `SchoolProfileForm.tsx`: (1) `handleLogoFile` had no try/catch — any rejected promise from `uploadSchoolLogo` left `uploadingLogo` stuck `true` forever with no error shown, matching the reported infinite "Uploading..." state; fixed by wrapping the upload in try/catch/finally (generic "Upload failed..." message on unexpected throw, `finally` always resets `uploadingLogo`). (2) The Logo `<FieldEditDrawer>` never passed `saving={uploadingLogo}` — "Done" stayed clickable mid-upload; fixed by adding the prop (the drawer already supported disabling on `saving`, just wasn't wired for this one field). **Underlying trigger found via dev server logs:** `Error: Body exceeded 1 MB limit` — Next's default Server Actions body limit (1MB) is below the app's own 2MB `MAX_LOGO_BYTES` cap, so any real logo roughly 1–2MB would throw before the app's own validation even ran. Fixed in `next.config.ts` — `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit: '3mb'` (fits the 2MB cap plus multipart overhead); dev server restarted to apply (config changes aren't hot-reloaded). **Separately discovered while re-verifying, NOT a code bug:** uploads of roughly >13KB on this machine return a Supabase `StorageApiError: Forbidden (403)` that looks identical to a real RLS rejection. Root-caused to the corporate Zscaler proxy, not Supabase or the app — confirmed two ways: (a) binary-searched the failure threshold with synthetic PNGs of identical 700×700 dimensions but varying byte size — 9.2KB passed, 13.6KB failed, ruling out image dimensions/content and pointing at raw request size; (b) the Supabase `school-logos` bucket's real `file_size_limit` is 2097152 bytes (2MB, matching `MAX_LOGO_BYTES` — queried directly via the Storage admin API with the service role key), far above the ~13KB failure point, so Supabase's own bucket config isn't the cause; (c) inspecting `@supabase/storage-js`'s error handling shows it falls back to the bare HTTP status text when a response body isn't valid JSON — "Forbidden" is the standard default reason phrase for 403, not a message Supabase's storage API actually sends for RLS violations (which read "new row violates row-level security policy..."); (d) the TLS certificate this machine sees for `supabase.co` is issued by "PayPal ZIA Issuing CA" / "Zscaler Inc." — full TLS interception, confirming Zscaler is positioned to inspect and block the POST body before it reaches Supabase. Conclusion: Zscaler's DLP/content-inspection is blocking larger multipart uploads on this local network only; this should **not** reproduce in production, since Vercel's servers don't route through this corporate proxy (same class of issue as [[local-dev-slowness-is-zscaler]]). Reverted the temporary `console.error` diagnostic added while investigating. **Not yet independently confirmed on a deployed preview URL / off-network** — recommend testing a real logo (typically several hundred KB to a few MB) via the live site or off the corporate network before considering this fully closed. **NOT committed.**

Given a choice between (a) keeping it read-only, (b) adding a stored-but-inert "target count" column just to hold a number Save writes to, or (c) building real add/remove-cycle reconciliation from scratch, **owner chose (a)** — no fake editable field with no real effect. (Sidebar per-workspace counts + "STREAM N today" footer still deferred — a separate query wired layout→Sidebar.) **Cross-page consistency fix (2026-09-20):** the Term panel's "Invoices issued / Students billed X/Y" disagreed with the cycle-detail page for the same term because `getDashboardKPIs` computed `studentsBilled` on a live-invoice, all-status-student basis while cycle-detail + the generator use an active-student, any-invoice basis. A withdrawn student's stray invoice could push billed past the active roll (numerator > denominator), and counting only non-cancelled invoices contradicted cycle-detail's "0 missing" (cancellation is sticky — the generator treats a cancelled-invoice student as already invoiced and won't re-issue). Aligned `dashboard.ts` to the same basis: `invoicesIssued` = every invoice row for the term; `studentsBilled` = distinct *active* students holding an invoice of any status; `unbilledCount` = active roll − billed. Added `students(status)` to the invoice select to enable the active filter. Live-verified the dashboard now reads "Invoices issued 128, Students billed 126 / 126, 0 unbilled" — exactly matching cycle-detail ("Invoices 128, all 126 invoiced") and the generator ("Generate for 0 new"); the misleading "N students with no invoice this term" needs-you row correctly disappears at 0. tsc clean. **Deep re-review (2026-09-20, standing loop) — built two designed states the first [x] pass silently omitted.** Re-drove `/dashboard` live (CDP + screenshots) against `App Shell.dc.html`'s `showToday` block (lines 188-320). The owner (populated) view was already faithful point-for-point (COLLECTED eyebrow + 54px ledger hero, %, three-segment bar that correctly collapses to two when nothing is overdue, "Needs you" money-at-risk queue, worst-first class chart, Record stream with green "received" lines, Term panel). Two permission-gated states the owner never reaches were missing in `dashboard/page.tsx`, both now built: (1) **`noTotals` panel** (canvas lines 209-219) — a viewer without `see-financial-totals` previously got an empty left column where the money hero would be; now shows "Money figures are not part of your role." with an honest explanation and "Ask the bursar or the owner to grant it." Rendered as a 2px ink left-rule with no fill (per [[no-caution-banners]], not the canvas's `#eae7e7`/`5px` tinted panel). Copy adapted to be true regardless of the viewer's other keys (does not claim they can see students/invoices) and avoids an em-dash per [[no-ai-slop-style]]. (2) **"Needs you" empty state** (canvas lines 254-259) — the section was omitted entirely when the queue was clear; now, when the viewer carries at least one queue permission, it renders "Needs you / CLEAR / Nothing is waiting on you." with a calm sub-line. Both states verified live by temporarily forcing the gates for the owner (`showFinancials=false` + empty queue), screenshotting, then reverting exactly (grep-confirmed no TEMP-QA markers left; owner regression re-screenshotted identical). **Considered decisions (reasoned, not omissions):** (a) the canvas's Term panel is always-shown, but the live Term panel stays gated on `showFinancials` — its "Invoices issued / Students billed" are aggregate figures, and widening that to non-financial roles is not clearly correct; kept the existing gate. (b) A viewer carrying **no** queue permission does not get the "Needs you" section at all (rather than a second "not yours" panel stacked under `noTotals`) — reasoned as calmer than the canvas's always-render, which in the mock only ever applies to curated roles. (c) The canvas's "Your view covers {todayScope}" line was omitted — the app has no per-viewer class-scoping concept, so any value would be fabricated. tsc clean.

The `activity_feed` view already exposes `parent_name`, `actor_name` (staff) and `amount`, so rather than shrink the copy the search was broadened to honour it: a shared `applySearch()` helper now ORs `student_name`/`parent_name`/`actor_name` `ilike` clauses plus an exact `amount.eq` when the term parses as a number (a naira sign / thousands commas are tolerated), used by the feed page, the count chips, the hero total and the CSV export alike. PostgREST `or()` structural characters (comma, parens, quotes, backslash) are stripped from the term first so a name like "Bello, Jr" can't break the filter; if nothing usable survives the query is left unfiltered. Stepper states exactly per owner brief: ACTIVE = filled ink block, white title, number hidden; COMPLETED = paper block, ink title, number in **ledger green `#0a6b3d`** (an explicit owner override of "green only where money arrived", for the completed-step marker only); UPCOMING = paper block, dim number + title; blocks in one 2px-ink-bordered strip, 2px ink separators, zero radius, horizontally scrollable at phone width. **02 Readiness checks** is a 3-column CHECK / WHAT IT MEANS / STATUS ledger driven by a new read-only `getYearEndReadinessAction` (current-session terms with status+end_date, pending-discount count, provider from `schools`) plus client facts (ladder health, carried balances): rows = term-to-roll-from (pass), class-promotion-ladder (**HARD block** only when 0 would promote AND >1 class exists — the all-graduate misconfiguration; otherwise info), unfinished-draft-term-in-session (soft "Allowed", ochre left-rule), term-dates-complete (soft), pending discount requests (soft, ochre, "Decide first" → `/discounts`), unpaid-balances-carry-forward (info), payment-provider (info); a HARD blocker disables Continue. **03 Promotion tree** is a master-detail ladder (owner scroll-fix, 2026-09-21): one compact row per class (source class → destination, count, `N promote · N repeat · N graduate` breakdown, exit classes ochre) is the primary view, so a clean year is confirmable at a glance; only ONE class expands at a time (`expandedClassId`, click a row to load its student override table in-place, chevron rotates, 2px ink left-rule marks the open class) so the page is never a scroll through every student — the collapsed view is ~1500px vs the old ~21,000px all-classes-expanded stack, and holds at 1000/400.         - **Nigerian scenario coverage matrix** (code-evidenced): (1) *Outstanding balance, promoted/repeating student* — COVERED: carries into the new session's first term via `previousBalance` at invoice-generation time (`computeInvoice.ts:185-304`), deferred not automatic; ledger previews count + ₦. (2) *Graduating class with outstanding balance* — BY DESIGN, now SURFACED: student -> `status='graduated'`, no new invoice, balance stays on the final now-closed invoice (carries nowhere, not written off); ledger states it explicitly with count + ₦ so it is a decision, not a silent loss. (3) *Repetition / held back* — COVERED: per-student override in promotion review; `repeat` keeps `currentClassId`, balance carries forward like a promote. (4) *Top class / graduation exit* — COVERED: `classes.next_class_id IS NULL` = exit point -> suggested `graduate` (`promotion.ts:59`). (5) *New-session fee setup* — COVERED: `createTerm` roll-forward copies per_term + deduped once_a_session fees, prices unchanged; adopt-a-prepared-term path uses its existing fees (ledger shows "Uses prepared term"). (6) *Non-last-term year-end attempt* — **GAP, see below.** (7) *Idempotency / double-run* — COVERED: `rollover_runs` step machine; Guard 1 (no concurrent run), Guard 2 (no re-run against an already-rolled `from_cycle`, "would double-promote"), resume advances per completed step. (8) *Already-withdrawn students* — COVERED: `getPromotionPreview` selects `status='active'` only; `carryForwardFeeAdjustments` carries active students only; withdrawn/graduated are excluded from promotion, carry-forward, and the ledger counts.
        - **Logic gaps flagged for owner sign-off (NOT built, per the no-risky-blind-fix rule):** (A) **Copy correction SHIPPED (safe, copy-only):** the canvas warn text "Run this once, after Third Term is closed" is wrong for this implementation — `startYearEndRollover` requires an `active` term and the rollover itself closes it (`createTerm(activateImmediately)` -> `closeTermAndCarryForward`, or the adopt path's explicit close), so closing the term first yields "No active term to roll over from". Warn line corrected to "Run this once, on the final term of the year while it is still active. The rollover closes that term itself, so do not close it beforehand." (B) **Non-last-term guard (real logic gap):** nothing verifies the active term is the *last* term of its session. Running year-end while First or Second Term is active would close that term, promote/graduate everyone, and jump straight to a brand-new session, silently skipping the remaining terms of the current year. Only the copy + type-to-confirm + the ledger's "New session opened: <name>" mitigate it. Recommend a soft-guard: derive the active term's position within its session (sibling-term count / `start_date` order) and warn or block when it is not the final term. (C) **Class-ladder sanity check:** if a school has not configured `classes.next_class_id`, *every* student defaults to graduate (seen live: 118 of 127 flagged graduate because the dev school's ladder is only partly set). It is visible in the ledger's graduate count and every row is overridable, but a rushed admin could mass-graduate a whole school. Recommend a pre-run warning when the graduate proportion is anomalous or most classes have a null `next_class_id`. So a fixed-₦ category shows the summed naira (ochre) and a %-based category shows the rule ("Varies"/"N%"), and the mode figure is the student count, not the canvas's ₦ total. A percentage discount's ₦ cost is `min(subtotal, subtotal * amount / 100)` — the exact formula `src/lib/discounts/compute.ts` uses at invoice-generation time, so the figure shown is real, not estimated. Both the group-level COST/TERM and each student's row now resolve to naira for fixed **and** percentage categories alike; a student with no active-cycle invoice yet is counted as "pending" and excluded from the sum rather than guessed at (group label appends "(N pending)"; that student's own row still shows the bare "N%" + "No invoice yet this term", so nothing is silently dropped). Low priority; would need to replicate the server's permission-superset comparison client-side to filter correctly. Not committed.

(3) Analytics drilldown + the Payments bundle were sending real numbers to the client regardless of permission — a real network-tab leak, not just a UI mask: `api/analytics/drilldown/route.ts` now checks `see-financial-totals` and zeroes `amountOwed`/`amountPaid` server-side; `getAnalyticsBundle()`'s result is now run through a new `redactAnalyticsBundle()` (zeroes billed/collected/outstanding/discountTotal/grossPotential/price across every series) in `payments/page.tsx` before it reaches the client `PaymentsDashboard`. **Known trade-off, flagged not fully solved:** this also flattens the rate/percentage visuals (collection rate %, bar-chart heights, fee price-fan lines) for this specific combination (`see-analytics` without `see-financial-totals`), since the client-side range-brushing math (`lib/analytics/aggregate.ts`) derives those ratios from the same raw numbers now zeroed — the catalog description says trend/rate visuals should show independent of the money permission, but doing that correctly needs the dashboard's interactive aggregation to work off pre-computed ratios instead of raw sums, a bigger change than this pass; revisit if this permission combination is actually used. (4) Recent Activity — `getRecentActivity` (dashboard widget) now redacts the payment line to "Payment received" (no ₦) when absent; `getActivityFeed`/`getActivityForExport` (full `/activity` page + its CSV export, the latter not explicitly flagged but sharing the same leak) now null the per-row `amount` (renders as the feed's existing `'—'`) and zero `aggregate.receivedInRange` server-side; `ActivityFeed.tsx`'s hero redacts to `'—'`. (5) Reports "Download history" (`getReportDownloads`) had zero filtering — now takes the same `showReports`/`showFinancials`/`showAuditLog` flags `ReportsLayout`'s own generate-a-report list already uses, filtered against `FINANCIAL_REPORT_TYPES`, so a viewer who reaches `/reports` via `see-audit-log` alone no longer sees financial report history. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Rebuilt (`CyclesLayout.tsx` only — no server-action / query / handler changes; `handleActivate`, `openEdit`, the structure-page link, `GenerateInvoicesPanel`, delete-draft and view-overview all preserved verbatim) around a `draftReadiness` memo deriving real prerequisites (`hasDates`, `feeSet` = feeItemCount > 0, `isPastSession` mirroring the server's session guard via the `sessions` prop; `ready = !isPastSession && dates && fees`): (1) **header pill states the state** — DRAFT · READY (ink) / DRAFT · N TO DO (ochre) / DRAFT · PAST SESSION (meta); (2) **steps grouped by real weight** into "REQUIRED BEFORE ACTIVATING" (dates, then fees) vs "OPTIONAL" (draft invoices) — a completed required step reads ink "SET" + outline button, an outstanding one reads ochre "REQUIRED" + an emphasized filled button, so the next action is obvious; optional invoices read dim; (3) **activation gated, not error-on-click** — moved into its own block (2px left ink rule, "Activate this term") with a plain-language real-numbers preview (which active term gets closed, its carry-forward ₦ + student count gated on `showFinancials`, how many active students this term will bill), and the "Activate term" button is now `disabled={!ready}` with an ochre reason line ("Do this first: add at least one fee." / the past-session message) instead of an always-enabled button that throws; (4) **green-for-"done" removed** from the draft + closed panels (ink for completed setup; ledger-green stays money-arrived only, per the DoD). Two prose em-dashes the rework introduced (the not-ready headline + the no-active-term preview) were changed to periods per [[no-ai-slop-style]]; the `—` nil-glyph in empty numeric table cells is kept (app-wide "no data" convention, matches the canvas). Live-verified via CDP at 1440: READY draft (`test term` / Third Term — ink SET steps, enabled Activate, preview names Second Term + ₦9,755,877 unpaid from 104 students carrying forward + "will bill 127 active students"), NOT-READY draft (a throwaway term, created to exercise the gate then deleted, DB left as found — DRAFT · 1 TO DO pill, step 02 ochre "REQUIRED" + filled "Add fees", disabled Activate + ochre "Do this first" reason), and the active/closed panels consistent (closed recap now reads ink "2 SET" / "8 INVOICED" / "CLOSED"). tsc clean; **NOT committed.** **Follow-up flagged (not built, owner decision):** the ACTIVE panel still uses ledger-green for non-money "DONE"/"ALL SENT" states and its generation/sent progress bars (a shared bar fill across all steps) — the same green-for-"done" DoD nuance fixed here for draft/closed; a correct bar fix needs a new per-step bar-colour field (a bigger change than this UX rework), so it was left for a dedicated pass. **Activate-term flow reworked to route through Close term first (2026-09-21, owner: "the activate term should not say ready until maybe all fees are corrected or no pending task like send invoices or generate them or recieve payments... it should just have the short description because the close term page does this. and the logic to activate a term closes the previous one so remember to take them to the close term page flow first then they close the term and we can activate the new one").** Two real gaps: (1) the "04 Activate this term" row's right-side pill was a bare word — READY (ledger green), NOT YET (ochre), PAST SESSION (meta) — driven only by the draft's own setup (dates/fees), saying nothing about whether the term that would actually get closed still had unsent/ungenerated invoices or unpaid balances; a school could see green "READY" while the live term it's about to close has 52 unsent invoices and 104 students who haven't paid. (2) `activateTerm` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts:608`) closes the currently-active term internally via `closeTermAndCarryForward` before activating the target — this was happening invisibly inside a plain `ConfirmDialog` ("X will be closed...") rather than through the dedicated Close term review flow (`/fees/close-term`, reached everywhere else via "Review close"). **Fix 1 — pill replaced with a factual description, not a verdict:** new `activeInvoiceGaps`/`activeTermPending` memos in `CyclesLayout.tsx` derive real facts from the active cycle (`totalActiveStudents - invoiceCount` not generated, `invoicesUnsent`, `invoicesNeedingResend`, `studentsWithOutstanding`); the pill is gone, replaced by one of: past-session message (meta), draft's-own-todo message (ochre, unchanged), a new "{active term} still has {facts} — activating routes you to close it first" (ochre, when the draft itself is ready but the live term isn't wrapped up), or "{active term} is fully wrapped up" / "Nothing else to do first" (meta) — matching the Close term page's own approach of stating facts instead of a pass/fail badge. Per the owner's explicit "it doesnt sto them from doing so," **none of this blocks activation** — the draft's own dates/fees gate is untouched, this only changes what's said about the *other* term. The description paragraph above it also gained a sentence naming the invoice gaps, and now states plainly "activating this closes it first — you'll review and confirm that separately." **Fix 2 — real routing through Close term:** `handleActivate` in both `CyclesLayout.tsx` and `CycleDetailLayout.tsx` (a second, independent copy of the same activate logic on the term-overview page, `/fees/cycles/[id]` — found and fixed for parity, since it has its own "Activate term" button reachable by clicking a term's name) now checks for a currently-active term different from the target; if one exists, it `router.push`es to `/fees/close-term?cycle={activeId}&activateAfter={draftId}` instead of opening a confirm dialog. `/fees/close-term/page.tsx` reads `?activateAfter`, validates the referenced cycle is still a draft, and passes it to `CloseTermLedger` as `activateAfter: {id, name}`. `CloseTermLedger.handleClose` calls the existing `closeTerm(cycle.id)` unchanged, and — only if `activateAfter` was passed — calls `activateTerm(activateAfter.id)` immediately after; since the active term is already closed by that point, `activateTerm`'s own "find currently active term" query returns null, so it activates directly with no second close and no double-carry-forward. UI additions: a one-line notice above the ledger ("Once this closes, {name} will be activated automatically"), the close button relabels to "Close {X} & activate {Y}", and the post-close result panel reads "{X} is closed — {Y} is now active" with a "View {Y}" primary link; an activation failure after a successful close is shown separately ("closed, but could not be activated: {error}. You can activate it from Cycles") rather than silently swallowed. When there's no currently-active term (first-ever activation), both pages fall back to the original direct `ConfirmDialog` → `activateTerm` flow unchanged. **Live end-to-end verified via CDP** on real (test) data: selected draft "test term" showed the new description ("Second Term still has 52 unsent, 52 changed since sending, 104 students yet to pay — activating routes you to close it first") in place of a bare READY pill; clicking "Activate term" routed to `/fees/close-term?cycle=...&activateAfter=...` showing "Once this closes, test term will be activated automatically" and a "Close Second Term & activate test term" button; clicking it produced "Second Term is closed — test term is now active," with the background carry-forward job chip tracking correctly. Same routing re-verified from the term-overview page's own Activate button. tsc clean; **NOT committed.**
