## ⚡ Scale & reliability

For schools of 500–1,000+ students on Vercel serverless. (No Monnify/messaging dependency — safe to build now.)
Still just Next.js + Supabase + Vercel throughout — no new infra needed. "Background job" here means: a
status/progress row in Supabase that the client polls, work picked up by a Vercel Cron-triggered route (or
simply a higher `maxDuration` on the route), not a separate server.

- [~] **Auth round-trip latency (per-click slowness)** — **partially fixed.** Every navigation used to make 4 sequential Supabase round-trips before page data: middleware `auth.getUser()` (network) + an `is_active` DB lookup, then `getAuthContext()` `auth.getUser()` **again** + a user/roles lookup. Done: (1) removed the redundant middleware `is_active` lookup (`getAuthContext` + `has_permission()` still enforce it); (2) both `auth.getUser()` → `auth.getClaims()` (local JWT verification once a session's token is ECC-signed). Project already uses **asymmetric ECC (P-256) signing keys** (legacy HS256 kept only as a previous key), so `getClaims` verifies locally — EXCEPT for sessions still carrying an older HS256 token, which fall back to a network `getUser` until the token refreshes/re-login. Region is confirmed optimal: Supabase `eu-central-1` (Frankfurt) == Vercel `fra1`, so prod round-trips are ~1–5ms; the ~800ms–1.1s seen in local dev is the corporate Zscaler proxy on the dev machine only, NOT representative of Vercel. Remaining: verify real timings on a Vercel deploy (non-Zscaler).
- [x] **Scale-up: compute tier + connection pooling** — **CLARIFIED 2026-09-16, no code change needed.** Re-checked the actual DB access pattern: the whole codebase talks to Postgres exclusively through `@supabase/ssr` / `@supabase/supabase-js` (PostgREST over HTTPS) — grepped for any raw `pg`/`DATABASE_URL`/`new Pool(...)` connection and found none. The pgBouncer/transaction-mode connection pooler only matters for code holding its own direct Postgres protocol connections (an ORM like Prisma, or the `pg` driver) — that's not this app, so there's no serverless-cold-start connection-storm risk to route around. PostgREST manages its own small, stable pool to the DB, sized by compute tier. The real (and only) lever before onboarding many schools is upgrading the Supabase **compute add-on** (currently `t4g.nano`, ~57%/56% compute/RAM at 1 school) — a dashboard/billing action, not a build task, consistent with the "gets faster on a paid tier with no code changes" design already followed elsewhere. Revisit — as a plan upgrade, not a PR — before onboarding beyond a handful of schools.
- [x] **`generateInvoicesForCycle` / `regenerateStaleInvoicesForCycle`** (`fees/cycles/actions.ts`) — **DONE 2026-09-05, hardened 2026-09-07.** Migrated to a shared `background_jobs` table (`db/background_jobs.sql`) + chunked worker route (`src/app/api/jobs/process/route.ts`, `src/lib/jobs/advanceJob.ts`) that resumes any job left `running` untouched — client starts the job then polls (`src/lib/jobs/pollJob.ts`, a fast 1.5s status read alongside the slow driver call), progress bar shows live `processed/total`. **2026-09-07:** the daily-only Vercel cron sweep meant a tab-closed job could sit frozen up to 24h — added `.github/workflows/job-sweep.yml` (free GitHub Actions cron, every ~5 min, no paid Vercel plan needed) hitting `/api/admin/job-sweep` with `SWEEP_SECRET`, and lowered `STALL_THRESHOLD_MS` to 3 min to match; worst-case resume time is now ~8 min instead of ~24h. Requires `APP_URL` + `SWEEP_SECRET` GitHub Actions repo secrets (added by the user, not automatable) — `APP_URL` is currently Vercel's stable `-git-dev-` branch alias since no production domain exists yet; swap to the real domain at launch. Vercel's own once-daily cron entry stays as a redundant fallback. **Also 2026-09-07:** `processInvoiceGenerationChunk`/`processInvoiceRegenerationChunk` (`src/lib/invoicing/invoiceGeneration.ts`) had the same N+1 as the staleness check, just on the write path — each of the 25 students in a chunk fired its own 5-7 queries, so a chunk could take 10-20+ seconds and the progress bar visibly jumped in batches instead of climbing smoothly. Fixed with the same `buildInvoiceComputePreload` batching (regeneration's credit-balance restore/spend is mirrored into the in-memory preloaded student record after each live mutation, since that field genuinely changes mid-loop unlike everything else preloaded). **2026-09-10:** added an in-app "job finished" notification — `src/lib/jobs/ActiveJobsProvider.tsx`, a context mounted once in `src/app/(app)/layout.tsx` (survives client-side navigation between pages, unlike the old per-modal poll loop) that drives the job's poll loop itself and fires a toast on completion no matter which page the user is on when it finishes. `GenerateInvoicesPanel` and the "Regenerate all" flow in `CycleDetailLayout` now delegate to it instead of polling locally, and the generation modal can be dismissed ("Run in background" button, or the always-visible close X) without stopping the job. Known gap: a full page reload while the modal is closed loses the visible progress bar for that page (jobId is component-local state), though the job itself keeps advancing and still toasts on completion — resuming the progress *bar* display across a hard reload would need the school's running jobs listed server-side on layout load, not yet built. Stress-tested end to end (chunking across 400 students/16 chunks, stalled-job sweep recovery, idempotent full-run, concurrent-start dedupe) against the real functions, not mocks. This is now the reference pattern for the three items below.
- [x] **CSV student import** — **DONE 2026-09-11 (`08e6a91`).** Moved onto the `background_jobs` pattern (`csv_import`): parse+validate up front, then `startCsvImportJob` enqueues the valid rows in `cursor.rows` and the worker/sweep chunks through them (`processCsvImportChunk`, 25/chunk) — survives tab-close and resumes. Stress-tested live: a 500-row file (16–17 deliberate error rows across every validation branch, 31 sibling families) imported 484 clean students end-to-end, per-school admission-number scoping confirmed, transactional 4,000-row bulk insert = 386ms.
- [x] **Bulk DVA creation** — **DONE 2026-09-11 (`513668e`), checklist bug fixed same day.** Replaced the 400-iteration client loop with a `bulk_dva` background job (`ensureBulkDVAJob` + `processBulkDVAChunk` + `advanceBulkDVA`), started by both the Settings "Create payment accounts" button and CSV import's phase 2 (the latter now chains **server-side** in `advanceCsvImport` on completion, so provisioning runs even if the tab closed mid-import). Failures now persist in the job row instead of client state. **Bug caught in review + fixed:** the first cut had no cursor — it decided completion by re-counting "students still missing a DVA," which a *failed* provisioning never removes, so any persistent failure looped the job (and the Monnify retries) forever and hung the UI. Fixed to carry a `cursor.studentIds` checklist like `csv_import`: each slice is crossed off whether it succeeds or fails, so the job always terminates and reports `created`/`failed` accurately.

- [x] **Cancel a running background job** — **DONE 2026-09-11 (`eb06ec6`).** All four job types (`invoice_generation`, `invoice_regeneration`, `csv_import`, `bulk_dva`) can now be stopped mid-run instead of only pausing on tab-close/timeout. `cancelJob()` (`backgroundJobs.ts`) flips `status='cancelled'` only from `'running'`; `updateJobProgress()` now scopes its own write to `status='running'` and returns whether it applied, so all 4 `advanceX` loops in `advanceJob.ts` notice a cancellation between chunks and stop without calling `completeJob`. New `/api/jobs/cancel` route mirrors `/api/jobs/process`'s session+permission auth (not a shared secret). `ActiveJobsProvider`'s existing global bottom-right progress indicator (not a new per-page button) got a small × per running job — one place covers all 4 job types instead of duplicating a button across `PaymentSettingsForm`/`CSVImportFlow`/`GenerateInvoicesPanel`/`CycleDetailLayout`. Each of those 4 completion handlers now has an explicit `'cancelled'` branch reporting "cancelled, N done" instead of either silently claiming success or rendering nothing. Cooperative, not instant — stops between chunks (25 items), so up to one chunk's worth of work (a few seconds to ~30s for provider-call-heavy chunks like DVA) still completes after the click.
- [x] **Surface incomplete/failed background jobs on login (server-side job status)** — **requested 2026-09-11, shipped 2026-09-14.** `(app)/layout.tsx` now queries `background_jobs` server-side for the signed-in school (`status in ('running','failed')`, `acknowledged_at is null`, `updated_at` within 24h) and hands them to `ActiveJobsProvider` as `interruptedJobs`: a still-`running` job re-attaches its live progress bar via the existing `drive()` polling (deduped by session, so no double-poll), and a `failed`/interrupted job shows a dismissible amber notice (processed/total/failed/error + a link back to the job's page) with a `background_jobs.acknowledged_at` column (`db/add_job_acknowledgment.sql` — **run this in the Supabase SQL editor**) and `/api/jobs/acknowledge` route so dismissing it is permanent. **Not built:** the optional "Retry" that re-enqueues remaining `cursor.studentIds` rather than restarting from zero — dismiss-and-redo-manually is the only path for now.
- [x] **Job-progress reopen: make it consistent + restorable** — **found while testing bulk_dva/bulk_send UX, 2026-09-14. DONE same day.** Three related gaps in the running-job UI, all fixed:
  1. **Inconsistent on-page reopen** — `bulk_dva` (`PaymentAccountsBanner.tsx`) and `bulk_send` (`InvoicesListLayout.tsx`) now use the same clickable-while-running `disabled` logic as `invoice_generation` (`disabled={panelOpen && running}`), so the on-page button reopens the panel for every job type instead of being disabled the whole time it runs. `InvoicesListLayout.tsx`'s send-all header also now stays visible while `sendRunning` even if `needsSend` drops to 0 mid-run (same fix already applied to the DVA banner).
  2. **Hidden chip can't be restored** — `ActiveJobsProvider.tsx` now tracks `hiddenRunningJobs` and renders a small "N hidden job(s) running — Show" pill above the chip stack whenever any exist; clicking it clears `hiddenChipIds` and brings every hidden chip back.
  3. **Cancel under-reports by ~one chunk** — `updateJobProgress()` (`backgroundJobs.ts`) now writes progress while `status` is `'running'` **or** `'cancelled'` (previously only `'running'`, so a cancel racing in right after a chunk finished its real work silently dropped that chunk's count) — a cancelled job's displayed `processed` now matches what's actually in the DB. Still true for `bulk_send` that an in-flight chunk really sends up to `CHUNK_SIZE` (25) messages before a cancel takes effect — the count is now honest, but the send itself isn't interruptible mid-chunk (unchanged, not addressed here).
- [x] **Cancel directly from the progress chip (cancel is currently gated behind a page load)** — **found while testing bulk_dva cancel, 2026-09-14.** Cancel only exists *inside* each job's modal, and the only way to reach that modal from the floating chip is to click it, which **navigates to the job's origin page** (`meta.href` — e.g. `/settings/payments` for a DVA job started from Settings) and waits for that whole page to load before the modal (and its Cancel button) appears. For a fast job — bulk_dva especially — the job **finishes during that navigation + load**, so the modal opens on an already-completed job and there's nothing left to cancel. Hit in practice: couldn't cancel a create-payment-accounts job because it completed before the Settings page finished loading. Fix: add an explicit, labelled **Cancel** control **on the chip itself** (`ActiveJobsProvider.tsx`) — `cancelJob(jobId)` is already in scope there via `useActiveJobs`, it just isn't wired to a chip button. Keep the existing **×** as "hide" (distinct from cancel) so the "corner-x-means-close" concern the current comment raises still holds — this is a *second*, clearly-labelled action, not a repurposed ×. Result: one-click cancel with no navigation, for every job type, regardless of which page started it or how slowly that page loads. Related: bulk_dva's chip routes to wherever the job was started (the Settings `PaymentSettingsForm` passes `href: '/settings/payments'`, the Students `PaymentAccountsBanner` passes `href: '/students'`), so "view progress" lands on different pages depending on origin — consider pointing the DVA chip consistently at the Students page (its primary home now). **FIXED 2026-09-16:** Added a labelled `aria-label="Cancel"` button next to the existing `aria-label="Hide"` × on every running job's chip in `ActiveJobsProvider.tsx`, calling the same `cancelJob(jobId)` already used by the per-page modals — no navigation involved. The button shows "Cancelling..." (disabled) once clicked, matching the modal's existing cancel-in-progress state. Live-tested end to end against the disposable Fees101 Scale Test School: started an `invoice_regeneration` job from the cycle detail page, navigated away immediately (unmounting the page's own cancel button) to `/dashboard`, confirmed the floating chip appeared with both a distinct Cancel and Hide control, clicked Cancel, confirmed the optimistic "Cancelling..." state, and confirmed the completion toast reported the job as cancelled with the correct partial-progress count — verified against the `background_jobs` row server-side, not just the UI toast. Script: `/Users/aadedeji/pw-test-fees101/walkthrough/60-cancel-chip-test.js`.
- [ ] **Bulk invoice sending** (`bulkSendInvoices`) — already the most defensive: batches of ≤50 + a 250ms per-send stagger to avoid slamming SMTP. Lower priority; a queue would mainly let the artificial stagger go away (parallel dispatch) and survive tab-close.
- [ ] **Reminders are 100% coupled to DVA provisioning** — **found during the 2026-09-16 payments walkthrough.** Manual "Send invoice"/"Send reminder" and the automated reminder sweep both hard-require a provisioned virtual account (the SMS body embeds the account number) — a school that hasn't finished payment-provider setup, or a student without a DVA yet, can't get any reminder at all, not even a plain "your invoice is ready, contact the school office" nudge with no account number in it. Worth a design decision later: whether a DVA-less fallback message is worth building, or whether "finish payment setup first" is an acceptable requirement.
- [ ] **Bulk SMS sending (reminders / "send to all now")** — **new, found during 2026-09-05 audit. Decided against building, 2026-09-16.** `sendDueRemindersForSchool` (`src/lib/messaging/reminders.ts`) and the message-sweep escalation loop (`src/app/api/admin/message-sweep/route.ts`) are plain synchronous `for` loops, one Sendchamp call per recipient, with zero chunking or time-budgeting — fine today since both only run from daily cron. Reconsidered whether a user-facing "send SMS to all now" button was actually needed: reminders already go out automatically per the cron schedule, and a per-student manual send already exists for one-off cases — there's no real gap this would close, so it's not being built. If that changes, the fix would be the same `background_jobs` pattern as invoice generation (new job_type, e.g. `bulk_sms`), not a raw loop, and Sendchamp must stay single-recipient per call regardless (bulk/array mode silently breaks per-message delivery tracking — see `src/lib/messaging/sendchamp.ts`).
- [x] **Bulk "Send all invoices" → background job** — **DONE 2026-09-11.** Replaced the buggy client-side `while (true)` loop (`bulkSendInvoices`, which recomputed "remaining" via a live query that a persistently-failing invoice never cleared, spinning forever and hammering Sendchamp) with a `bulk_send` background job. Core per-invoice send logic extracted to `src/lib/invoicing/sendInvoice.ts` (`sendInvoiceCore` — no permission check or per-item audit log, mirrors `provisionStudentDVA`'s split); `startBulkSendInvoicesJob` snapshots every pending invoice ID into `cursor.invoiceIds` up front (same fixed-checklist pattern as `bulk_dva`), `processBulkSendChunk` consumes it a chunk at a time via `advanceBulkSend` (`advanceJob.ts`), keeping the existing 250ms per-send stagger. Single-invoice "Send to parent" (`invoices/[id]/actions.ts`) now calls the same `sendInvoiceCore`, so both paths share one implementation. `InvoicesListLayout.tsx` moved onto the standard `trackJob`/`useTrackedJob` pattern (cancel button, tab-close resilience, honest cancelled/failed reporting) shared with the other 4 job types. Requires running `db/add_bulk_send_job_type.sql` once (adds `bulk_send` to the `job_type` check constraint) before use.
- [x] **`closeTermAndCarryForward` → background job** — **DONE 2026-09-14, pending browser test.** `closeTermAndCarryForward` (`src/app/(app)/fees/cycles/actions.ts`) still does the synchronous part inline (close the cycle, compute outstanding students/total, find future-term invoices for them — none of that risks a timeout), but the actual per-invoice recompute loop (`applyCreditBalanceDelta` undo → `computeInvoiceForStudent` → `invoices` update → re-apply credit) moved to a new `close_term` `background_jobs` type, extracted into `processCloseTermCarryForwardChunk` (`src/lib/invoicing/closeTermCarryForward.ts`) and driven by a new `advanceCloseTerm` in `src/lib/jobs/advanceJob.ts`. The function returns its summary (`studentsWithOutstanding`, `totalOutstanding`, queued `invoicesUpdated`/`invoicesNeedingResend` counts) immediately as before — all three callers (`closeTerm`, `activateTerm`, the year-end rollover's `adoptCycleId` continuation) keep working unchanged — plus a `jobId` when there was enough work to hand off. `CyclesLayout.tsx` tracks that job via `trackJob` so the floating chip/toast shows live progress and completion, same as every other bulk job. DB migration: `db/add_close_term_job_type.sql` (adds `close_term` to the job_type check constraint — run before testing). Not yet tested in the browser (need a school with a closing term, outstanding balances, and future-term invoices spanning 2+ chunks to see it actually go through the job path instead of the `jobId: null` skip-if-nothing-to-do case).
- [x] **Year-end rollover** — already has checkpoint/resume infra (`rollover_runs`/`rollover_promotions`, a `step` state machine) to *recover* after a timeout, but the happy path still runs as one giant call, and resume is still a manual "Resume rollover" button click (`YearEndRolloverWizard.tsx`) after a visible failure — no auto-retry or cron sweep for a stalled run (unlike `background_jobs`' daily sweep). Upgrade: drive the state machine step-by-step via a Cron-triggered route with the client polling `getRolloverStatus` automatically while `status === 'in_progress'`, so a timeout can't happen at all instead of just being recoverable from, and a stalled run doesn't need a human to notice and click. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** Added `src/app/api/admin/rollover-sweep/route.ts`, mirroring `job-sweep`'s pattern — finds any `rollover_runs` row `status = 'in_progress'` whose `updated_at` is older than a 3-minute stall threshold and resumes it directly via `continueYearEndRolloverForSweep` (same step machine the manual button drives), protected by `SWEEP_SECRET`/`CRON_SECRET` like the other admin sweep routes. `.github/workflows/job-sweep.yml` now also pings this endpoint on its existing ~5 minute GitHub Actions cadence, and `vercel.json` got a matching once-daily `/api/admin/rollover-sweep` entry as a fallback. `YearEndRolloverWizard.tsx` now auto-polls `getRolloverStatus` every few seconds while `status === 'in_progress'`, so a resumed run's progress shows up without the user needing to click anything. Requires a new column: `db/rollover_runs_updated_at.sql` adds `updated_at` (defaulted, trigger-maintained via the existing `set_updated_at()` function) plus a partial index for the sweep's lookup — **not yet applied, needs to be run in the Supabase SQL editor** before the sweep route can do anything (until then it 400s harmlessly on the missing column, per the route's own comment). Live-tested the auth guards and pre-migration no-op safety by curling `/api/admin/rollover-sweep` directly (missing/wrong secret rejected, correct secret accepted and returns cleanly). Deliberately did NOT live-test a full stalled-run resume: that needs the migration applied first, and running an actual rollover against the disposable Fees101 Scale Test School would have destroyed the seeded 56-invoice cycle fixture the pagination item below depended on. Also did not touch "Greenfield Comprehensive Academy" (a separate, ongoing walkthrough project) or run any rollover action against it, per the standing safety constraint for this school.

Config-only, no behavior change — confirmed via `tsc --noEmit --pretty` and a live app smoke check.
- [ ] **When upgrading Vercel's plan: bump `JOB_TIME_BUDGET_MS`** (`src/lib/jobs/backgroundJobs.ts`, currently 50s) — deliberately conservative for Vercel's free-tier function duration ceiling. A paid plan allows much longer function runtimes, but the code won't use the extra headroom until this constant is raised to match — a one-line, low-risk change, do it in the same conversation as the plan upgrade, not preemptively (setting it too high before upgrading risks a mid-chunk kill). Vercel's concurrent-execution ceiling and Supabase's connection pool/compute tier (the more likely bottleneck at ~20 schools running big jobs at once — see the connection pooling item above) scale automatically with a paid plan, no code change needed.
- [x] **Server-side pagination** on the Students page (currently loads all students + a big `.in()` lookup; sluggish at 400+) — deliberately NOT applied to the audit log, which already paginates server-side (`getAuditLog` via Supabase `.range()`) since that log is permanent/unbounded, unlike the bounded per-school student list. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** `src/lib/queries/students.ts`'s `getStudents()` now takes `page`/`perPage` and applies Supabase `.range()` at the DB level (both the single-query fast-sort path and the two-pass class-name-sort path), returning `total` alongside the page slice. `src/app/(app)/students/page.tsx` parses `page`/`perPage`/`search`/filters from `searchParams` and passes them through; `StudentsTable.tsx` holds only a debounced search buffer locally and drives every other change (page, per-page, filters, sort) through a `navigate()` helper that merges into the URL and does `router.push()`, with a `PAGE_SIZE_OPTIONS` constant defined locally in the client component rather than imported from the query module. Live-tested against the disposable Fees101 Scale Test School seeded with 70 students: verified page 1/2 navigation, per-page size changes, search + filter combined with pagination, and sort-column changes all reset to page 1 and hit the DB with the correct `.range()` bounds (confirmed no full-table fetch).
- [x] **Server-side pagination on the billing cycle detail page's invoice list** — **noted 2026-09-07**, found while stress-testing invoice generation at 400 students. Same shape as the Students page issue above — build both in the same pass when this work starts. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** Unlike the Students page, pagination here is a presentation-layer slice rather than a DB-level `.range()` — the cycle's staleness check (`needsRegeneration`/`regenerationBlocked`) and KPI totals (`totalsByStatus`, `totalExpected`, `totalOutstanding`, `lockedOutOfDate`, `autoRegenerableCount`) require a per-invoice `computeInvoiceForStudent` recompute that can't be pushed into a SQL aggregate, so `getCycleDetailById()` (`src/lib/queries/fees.ts`) still fetches and recomputes every invoice in the cycle, then filters/paginates the already-computed in-memory array before returning it — KPIs and the staleness banner stay correct and identical regardless of which page is displayed. New `GetCycleDetailOptions` (`filter`/`search`/`page`/`perPage`) and expanded `CycleDetailData` return shape (`invoicesTotal`, `page`, `perPage`, `lockedOutOfDate`, `autoRegenerableCount`, `studentsWithoutInvoicesTotal`). `src/app/(app)/fees/cycles/[id]/page.tsx` parses and validates `searchParams`; `CycleDetailLayout.tsx` was converted from local `useState`/`useMemo` filtering to the same URL-driven `navigate()` pattern as `StudentsTable.tsx`, including a locally-defined `CYCLE_INVOICES_PAGE_SIZE_OPTIONS` constant and `import type` for everything pulled from `fees.ts` (a real value import there was tried first and broke the client build — see below). `studentsWithoutInvoices` (active students with no invoice this cycle) stays a deliberately unpaginated list, shown in full whenever the filter is `all`/`no_invoice`; blending pagination across two differently-shaped data sources (invoices vs. missing-invoice students) was judged unnecessary complexity for this scope, at the accepted cost that the same "no invoice" rows repeat at the bottom of every invoice page rather than paginating separately. Caught and fixed a real Next.js/Turbopack build break during this work: a `'use client'` component importing a real value (not just a type) from a module that transitively depends on `next/headers` (`fees.ts` → `discounts.ts` → `computeInvoice.ts` → `supabase/server.ts`) pulls that entire server-only module graph into the client bundle — fixed by switching to `import type` and defining the page-size options locally, mirroring `StudentsTable.tsx`'s existing workaround. Live-tested against the disposable Fees101 Scale Test School's seeded 56-invoice cycle (`d160179f-7985-4d77-8b37-37050c6c819b`): default page load, page 2 navigation with KPIs/staleness banner byte-identical across pages, a per-page size covering the whole cycle in one page, filter tabs (confirmed `paid` filter returns only paid-status rows), search combined with pagination, filter/per-page changes both resetting to page 1, Next/Previous controls, and a live "Regenerate one" action confirming `router.refresh()` correctly preserves the URL-derived page/filter/search state (since none of it is local `useState` anymore). Script: `/Users/aadedeji/pw-test-fees101/walkthrough/70-cycle-pagination-test.js`.
- [x] **Fix N+1 in `computeInvoiceForStudent`** — **DONE 2026-09-07.** The billing-cycle detail page's staleness check (`src/lib/queries/fees.ts`) was calling `computeInvoiceForStudent` sequentially once per invoice, and each call fired 5-7 of its own sequential queries — ~2,000-2,800 round trips at 400 invoices, the dominant cause of slow term-page loads (bigger than the pagination gap above). Added `buildInvoiceComputePreload()` (`src/lib/computeInvoice.ts`) fetching everything cycle-wide in ~8-9 batched `.in()` queries, threaded through an optional `preload` param on `computeInvoiceForStudent`/`computeDiscountsForInvoice`/`computeSiblingDiscount`/`getRecurringDiscounts` — all 8 other call sites omit it and are unaffected. Math is byte-identical to before, just sourced from preloaded maps instead of live queries. **2026-09-11 follow-up:** the preload was incomplete — `computeDiscountsForInvoice`'s three discount lookups (`manualByInvoice`, `recurringByStudent`, `siblingsByFamily`) treated a *missing* map entry as "not loaded" and fell back to a per-invoice query, which fired for every invoice with no discount (the common case). Measured: the staleness loop still took ~21.6s for 279 invoices (~1 query each). Fixed in `src/lib/discounts/compute.ts` to treat a present preload as authoritative (absent entry = empty, never query). Re-timed on the same 279-invoice term: preload 861ms + loop **2ms** = 863ms total (was ~281s projected on the pure per-invoice path), 0 invoices falsely flagged. Now scales flat (~1s at 4,000). If you touch this again, keep the rule: when `preload` is passed, NEVER fall back to a per-item query.
- [x] **Live page updates via Supabase Realtime** — **planned 2026-09-07, DONE 2026-09-16.** Every page was server-rendered, fetch-once; a payment landing via webhook (Monnify/Paystack) or another staff member's edit only showed up after a manual refresh. Audited RLS first on every table before subscribing (Realtime replays through the same per-subscriber RLS as normal reads) against the `fees101_schema.sql` snapshot — `invoices`/`payments`/`discounts`/`students`/`message_logs`/`audit_log`/`users`/`classes`/`families` all scope SELECT by `school_id = current_school_id() OR is_super_admin()`, no `USING (true)` policy anywhere, both helper functions exclude deactivated users — no RLS gap found. Built one reusable client hook, `useRealtimeRefresh(subscriptions)` (`src/lib/realtime/useRealtimeRefresh.ts`), that subscribes to `postgres_changes` per `{table, filter}` pair and debounces `router.refresh()` (~450ms after the last event in a burst, so e.g. 400-invoice generation doesn't fire hundreds of refreshes on an open tab). Wired into: Dashboard, Invoices list + invoice detail, Student detail, Cycle detail, Payments analytics page, Recent Activity feed, Discounts page — each subscribed to the specific tables/filters relevant to that page (school_id-scoped, or narrower by invoice_id/student_id/billing_cycle_id where the page is already scoped that way). Pages without an existing client Layout to hook into got a small dedicated wrapper component (`DashboardRealtimeRefresh.tsx`, `StudentRealtimeRefresh.tsx`, `DiscountsRealtimeRefresh.tsx`); the rest have `useRealtimeRefresh` called directly inside their existing client Layout. New migration `db/enable_realtime.sql` (sets `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL` on each table, then `ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE ...` for `invoices`/`payments`/`discounts`/`message_logs`/`students`/`audit_log`) — the `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL` is not optional here: the subscriptions filter on non-PK columns (`school_id`/`student_id`/`invoice_id`/`billing_cycle_id`), and without it Postgres only logs the primary key on UPDATE/DELETE, so both the filter and the (school_id-based) RLS would drop an "invoice went paid" UPDATE or a cancellation DELETE for filtered subscribers; INSERTs already carry the full row so they'd work either way. **Not yet run, needs to be applied manually in the Supabase SQL editor** before any of this actually receives events (until then the subscriptions sit idle, no error, just no live updates). Not yet browser-tested with two sessions per page — needs that plus the migration before considering this fully verified. **Extended to whole-app coverage 2026-09-16:** after the user confirmed Realtime working (a service-role `apply_payment_to_invoice` call against the Paystack test school updated an open page live), coverage was pushed to every server-rendered page that shows externally-mutable data so nothing needs a manual refresh. Added a single reusable wrapper `RealtimeRefresh.tsx` (takes `subscriptions` as a prop; the three older bespoke wrappers stay as-is) and mounted it on: students **list**, fees overview, fees/cycles list, fee structure, year-end rollover (live job progress via `rollover_runs`), reports, and settings pages audit-log, users, roles-permissions, academic-structure, payments, data-privacy, discounts, reminders, and the settings index. `db/enable_realtime.sql` now also publishes `billing_cycles`, `fee_items`, `student_fee_adjustments`, `classes`, `sections`, `sessions`, `users`, `roles`, `schools`, `families`, `report_downloads`, `school_deletion_requests`, `rollover_runs` (all RLS-re-audited: each has RLS enabled + a school-scoped SELECT policy; `schools` is filtered on `id`, everything else on `school_id`), and the publication-membership step was rewritten as an idempotent `DO` loop so the whole file is safe to re-run (the original six are already members). Two subagents verified the wiring: per-page correctness (all 15 PASS — correct filter column per table, guarded on truthy school id, every subscribed table published) and coverage completeness (no gaps; `students/import` and `settings/account-security` correctly need none). Two known, deliberate caveats: (1) the config-form pages (reminders, discounts, settings index) are wired to `schools` but their forms hold values in local React state, so a concurrent admin's edit won't visibly update an open form until reload — wired for consistency, low real-world payoff; (2) `DrilldownModal` (a client component under the payments dashboard) fetches its own data in a `useEffect`, so if left open exactly when a webhook lands it won't re-fetch — transient user-opened modal, flagged as an optional follow-up, not built. **Still needs the updated migration re-run in Supabase + two-session browser testing to be fully verified.** **CONFIRMED WORKING 2026-09-24:** owner ran `db/enable_realtime.sql`; dashboard's Publications page confirmed `supabase_realtime` correctly lists all 19 tables with INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE enabled. Live-verified via the dev-only `/api/dev/simulate-payment` endpoint (real code path — `applyProviderPayment`, same as a genuine webhook) against the Paystack Test School's student "David Adeyemi" while the owner watched an open Student Detail tab: the `<StaleDataStrip>` correctly appeared ("These figures changed while you were reading" / "Load 2 changes") within ~1s of each simulated payment, with no manual refresh — confirming `postgres_changes` events are now actually delivered end-to-end (subscribe → filter → RLS → client). The earlier 2026-09-23 zero-events result was a genuinely un-run migration, not a code bug. **Owner then explicitly reversed the "manual load" UX 2026-09-24** ("I do not want to have them click load 2 changes... it should be realtime and live and they shouldn't need to refresh") — overrides the canvas's STALE DATA state design in favour of true auto-refresh. `useRealtimeRefresh` now calls `router.refresh()` itself on the debounced event (back to the pre-Phase-08 shape) instead of returning `{pendingCount, applyRefresh}`; deleted the now-fully-unused `StaleDataStrip.tsx` and stripped its import/render from all 9 call sites (`RealtimeRefresh.tsx` + the 3 bespoke wrappers + `PaymentsDashboard`/`InvoicesListLayout`/`CycleDetailLayout`/`InvoiceDetailLayout`/`ActivityFeed`). `tsc --noEmit --pretty` clean. Live-verified: two more simulated payments against "David Adeyemi" auto-updated the open Student Detail tab with no click. Not committed.

## 🏢 Platform / owner (multi-tenant) — your admin side

No owner-facing product surface exists yet. Schools are created directly in the DB; signup makes everyone a `super_admin`.

- [ ] **Platform dashboard research (2026-09-24) — business-model decisions needed before any of this is built.** Owner asked for full research (billing model, suspension, audit, comms, integrations, manual overrides, UI shape) before building anything, per "no risky blind fixes." Recommendations made, none committed: **billing** — per-student per-term (matches infra cost + market norm), price point and "active student" definition still open; **cadence** — bill on term-activation (`activateTerm` hook), not fixed-calendar, unless owner wants calendar predictability instead; **collection** — manual invoice + bank transfer for v1, auto-deduct from the school's own DVA collections explicitly deferred (highest blast-radius option here — needs its own consent/legal design pass, not folded into v1); **suspension** — graduated ladder (grace notice → soft/read-only → hard lockout), not straight to hard lockout, exact day-counts and manual-vs-automatic trigger still open; **audit** — needs a *second*, platform-level audit trail (founder/internal actions across tenants: suspend, edit billing, impersonate, clear DVA) separate from the existing per-school `/settings/audit-log`; **comms** — reuse existing Brevo/Sendchamp infra with a new message type for school-facing (not parent-facing) notices, ties into the already-tracked "Admin dashboard for message delivery status" item; **integrations visibility** — per-school payment provider/DVA status, webhook health, credential validity, SMS/email volume — surfacing existing data (`message_logs`, `payment_provider`, DVA columns), no new third-party connections needed; **manual overrides** — DVA clear after provider switch, deletion restore/cancel, manual-payment toggle, plan edits, suspend/reactivate — every one must write to the new platform audit trail; **shape** — tenant directory, per-school detail view, billing overview, platform audit log, internal team/permissions (v2, already scoped below). **Open decisions only the owner can make:** price per student, exact suspension day-counts + automatic vs. manual trigger, whether auto-deduction from collections is in scope now or deferred.

**Resolved 2026-09-25 — cadence and collection mechanism:** owner correctly flagged that flat per-term billing is unfair (a 4-term school pays more per year than a 3-term school for the same student count) and that monthly billing mismatches how schools actually collect from parents (in termly lumps, not monthly — a monthly auto-debit would mostly hit dry accounts). **New cadence (corrected 2026-09-25 — owner caught a flaw: student counts move term to term, so nothing about population should be locked):** price annually per student. Normalize only the *rate*, never the headcount — per-term unit rate = annual price ÷ that school's terms-per-year (already recorded in their academic structure), so a 4-term school's per-term rate is proportionally smaller than a 3-term school's and their annual totals match. The actual per-term charge = that unit rate × the school's real active-student-count read fresh at the moment of `activateTerm` (existing hook, no new scheduling engine needed) — never a count decided or locked earlier in the year. This means enrollment growth/shrinkage between terms is automatically reflected each time, with no reconciliation step needed. Only the term-count-per-year is treated as fixed for the academic year (since it rarely changes mid-year, unlike enrollment) — everything about student count stays live. **Collection mechanism:** owner wants to use Paystack billing agreements. Important technical distinction surfaced (not yet live-verified — web tools were network-blocked in this environment when checked): Paystack's Subscriptions/Plans object is fixed-amount/fixed-interval and doesn't fit a variable per-student, variable-timing charge; the correct primitive is charging a saved reusable authorization code (`charge_authorization`) on demand — capture consent once via an initial card authorization per school, then the app decides amount + timing per term-activation. **Before building:** confirm this against Paystack's actual current docs/dashboard (a live web check, not blocked-tool guesswork), and design the failure path (declined/expired card) so it feeds directly into the suspension ladder above rather than silently failing.

**Schema/codebase verification (2026-09-25, read-only research agent, nothing built):** checked the design against what actually exists before committing to it. Two real prerequisites found, not yet true today: (1) **no `terms_per_year` field exists anywhere** — `sessions`/`billing_cycles` (`fees101_schema.sql:3336-3351, 3836-3845`) let you count terms already created, but that's only known retroactively (mid-year, you can't count terms that haven't been created yet) — needs an explicit per-school-per-session field set up front, not derived. (2) **`activateTerm` (`fees/cycles/actions.ts:609-724`) is a clean single hook point, but has a bypass**: `createTerm`'s `activateImmediately: true` path activates a term directly without calling `activateTerm` — a billing hook placed only there would miss new-term-created-and-activated-at-once. Both paths need the hook, or activation needs a shared helper. **Reusable groundwork found:** `schools.subscription_status` (`active | payment_due | suspended | inactive | cancelled`) and `subscription_started_at` already exist as unused columns (`fees101_schema.sql:3775-3790`) — map almost directly onto the suspension ladder, apparently anticipated but never wired up. Payment credential storage is cleanly separate: the school's own Paystack/Monnify keys (for collecting from parents) live on `schools.provider_api_key`/`provider_secret_key`; a founder-bills-school feature needs entirely new storage (a founder-side Paystack customer code + authorization code per school) since nothing like that exists today.

**Dashboard shape addition (2026-09-25) — cost-to-serve per school, for usage-based billing decisions:** owner wants per-school insight into SMS/email volume + cost and storage consumed, specifically to spot "heavy loaders" and decide whether a school's plan/price should reflect above-average usage. Checked against the schema — this is mostly free: `message_logs` already has `channel` (`sms | whatsapp | email`) and `cost_amount` (numeric) per row, so per-school volume and spend by channel is a straight `SUM(cost_amount) GROUP BY school_id, channel` — no new tracking needed, just a query + a dashboard table/chart. **Real gap:** no byte-level storage accounting exists anywhere — `dataPrivacy/inventory.ts` counts *rows* per table (students, invoices, message_logs, etc.) for the transparency/export feature, not bytes; attachment files (`message_logs.attachment_url` and any Supabase Storage bucket objects) aren't sized or aggregated per school anywhere. "Storage a school is taking up" would need either a new periodic job that sums Supabase Storage object sizes per school-scoped folder/prefix, or treating row counts as a rough proxy (cheaper, less accurate) until real usage patterns are known. Folded into the dashboard-shape scope above as a new **usage & cost-to-serve** view: per-school SMS/email count + spend by channel (buildable now), storage consumed (needs new accounting), surfaced next to the billing overview so a heavy-usage school's plan can be revisited deliberately rather than discovered after the fact.

- [~] **Onboarding setup sequence (dependency order)** — design the actual step-by-step order a new school must configure things in — e.g. academic structure/classes before fee structure before students before invoice generation — so self-onboarding can't skip a step that a later one silently depends on. This is separate from the tenant-creation mechanics above and from the setup-guide-mascot UI (line 239 area) — it's the underlying dependency map those two features need to already exist before they can guide/validate anything. **Dependency map written 2026-09-16: `docs/onboarding-sequence.md`** (8 phases, grounded in actual page routes/actions, plus the known silent-failure gaps a guided flow should catch). Still `[~]` not `[x]` — no guided UI or validation enforces any of this yet; that's the remaining work once the portal's visual redesign happens.
- [ ] **Self-onboarding flow, designed 2026-09-24 for the ~5-school scale-up** — researched current state first (2 read-only agents: full codebase trace + re-read of `docs/onboarding-sequence.md`) before designing anything, per the "no risky blind fixes" rule, since this directly reverses a recorded product decision. **Confirmed current reality:** `/signup` was already fully deleted (not just gated) on 2026-09-16 after it was found to escalate every new user to `super_admin` with `school_id: null` — a real privilege-escalation bug, not a hypothetical. `middleware.ts` now hard-redirects any `/signup` hit to `/login` with the comment "schools are onboarded by Fees101." **This plan does NOT reopen public self-signup** — that would reintroduce exactly the vetting/security gap the deletion fixed, for a payments product where a fake school could otherwise provision real bank accounts. Instead: keep tenant *creation* founder-gated, make everything *after* that fully self-serve, which is the actual bottleneck at 5 schools (SQL-editor toil per school, not a lack of a public signup page).
  - **Phase 0 — the real gap, currently zero code.** Reconstructed the manual process today: founder hand-inserts a `schools` row in the Supabase SQL editor, then hand-creates the owner's Supabase Auth user + matching `public.users` row (`role: 'school_admin'`). Nothing in the repo automates any of it. One piece already **is** automated — `db/seed_default_roles_trigger.sql`'s `AFTER INSERT ON schools` trigger auto-seeds the Administrator/Bursar roles — so a manually-inserted row is already role-complete today; a Phase 0 build just needs to insert the row and stop doing everything else by hand.
    - Build a single founder-only action (`super_admin`-gated, e.g. `/internal/schools/new`, or even a CLI-style script if a UI isn't worth it yet at 5 schools): takes school name + owner email, inserts the `schools` row, then reuses the **exact same `inviteUserByEmail` pattern `addStaff` already uses** for the owner user (`role: 'school_admin'`, `school_id` set to the new row) — this is almost entirely wiring together two things that already exist (`addStaff`'s invite mechanics + the schools-insert the SQL editor currently does by hand), not new infrastructure. The owner receives the same invite email → set-password flow every staff invite already uses today.
    - **Prerequisite bug to fix as part of this, not after:** `loadAuthContext()` resolves a `super_admin` with `school_id: null` to "whichever school is first in the table" (`src/lib/auth/permissions.ts`) — a harmless accident at 1 school, actively dangerous at 5 (the founder's own internal tooling could silently act against the wrong tenant). Fix before or alongside Phase 0: either give the founder's `super_admin` account an explicit "no school context" state for internal-only actions, or require the new school-creation action to take no ambient school context at all (it shouldn't need one — it's creating one).
  - **Phase 1 onward — already self-serve, confirmed by the research pass, nothing new to build:** academic structure, sessions/terms, fee structure, students (single + CSV import), payment provider connection + DVA provisioning (bulk + auto-on-add), and staff invites are all real in-app UI today, gated only by permissions, no founder involvement past Phase 0. The gap here isn't *capability*, it's *guidance* — nothing stops a school from doing these out of order or skipping one silently (see the 5 failure modes `docs/onboarding-sequence.md` already lists: ₦0 invoices from an empty fee structure, invoices sent before a payment provider is connected, blank DVA account numbers on late-added students, etc.).
    - Build the **guided setup checklist** the roadmap has been calling "setup-guide-mascot" — at 5-school scale this should be a thin progress rail, not new CRUD screens: a persistent sidebar/dashboard panel that deep-links into the existing pages in `docs/onboarding-sequence.md`'s order, with each step's "done" state read from real data (e.g. "Add your first class" ticks once `classes` count > 0 for that school) rather than a separate progress table to keep in sync.
    - Turn the doc's 5 known silent-failure gaps into actual soft gates while building this, since they're already fully scoped: warn (don't hard-block — an experienced admin should still be able to override) before activating a term with zero fee items; warn before the first bulk invoice send if no payment provider is connected/DVAs aren't provisioned; flag students missing a DVA after CSV import instead of leaving it silently blank; surface the sibling phone-matching caveat inline when a school has sibling discounts configured.
  - **Deliberately NOT part of this pass, even at 5 schools:** tenant directory, impersonation/school-switcher UI, per-tenant billing/usage metering — all still correctly listed further down this section as unbuilt; 5 schools is small enough for the founder to track by name, and building tenant-management UI now would be solving a problem that doesn't exist yet. Revisit once school count makes "whichever school is first in the table" tooling gaps recur elsewhere, or once a second Fees101-side staff member needs their own scoped internal access.
  - **Sequencing:** Phase 0 (tenant creation action + the `super_admin` fallback fix) should ship first and alone — it's the only piece blocking onboarding a 2nd/3rd/4th/5th school at all today. The guided checklist is a quality-of-life layer on top of capability that already exists, so it can follow once Phase 0's out, and naturally waits for the in-progress visual redesign anyway (per `docs/onboarding-sequence.md`'s own note that the step map "will need a fresh pass once the portal is visually redesigned").
  - **Sequencing reconsidered 2026-09-25 (owner):** don't build Phase 0 automation yet. Onboard the next 2-3 schools manually (as today) first, and study what each one actually brings/needs during that process — real-world variation in academic structure, fee patterns, staff setup habits — so the guided checklist and the founder-onboarding action are designed against real cases instead of guessed ones. The manual SQL-editor toil for 2-3 more schools is accepted as the cost of that research. Revisit Phase 0 once that hands-on pattern is understood. Owner separately flagged Paystack/Monnify integration work as the hardest, most time-consuming part of onboarding a school today — worth watching closely during these next few manual onboardings, since it may end up shaping Phase 0 more than the schools-table/staff-invite mechanics do.

The school does NOT self-cancel; recovery is admin-side. Until this dashboard exists, the interim cancel path is a secret-protected admin route / manual DB update on `school_deletion_requests`.

Discussed 2026-09-14: build a reversible "hide" instead of a true delete, for the mistaken-CSV-import case — likely admin-only (owner/super_admin) so it can't casually be used to make data disappear from a bursar's view. Decide at build time whether this ships standalone or waits for the admin dashboard.
- [ ] **Payment provider switch (Paystack ↔ Monnify) — admin-only DVA clear** — discussed 2026-09-14. Today `getPaymentProviderForSchool` resolves one provider per school off a single `payment_provider` column; a school can't run two providers at once, and bulk DVA creation only targets students with `provider_dva_reference IS NULL` — so if a school's provider is switched, existing students silently keep their old (now-unreconciled) account number instead of getting a new one. This is deliberately an owner-only action, not something a school self-serves: from the admin dashboard, the owner clears the affected school's DVA fields (`provider_dva_reference` + the related columns) after switching `payment_provider`, which makes the existing "Create accounts" bulk-DVA job pick every student back up under the new provider. One account active at a time — switching back later just creates a fresh one again, no special-casing needed. *Depends on the owner/admin dashboard existing at all (line 160) — until then, a manual DB clear is the interim path, same pattern as the deletion-recovery item above.* **v2, later:** consider letting a school hold two simultaneous DVAs (one per provider) in case they want to switch back — needs a real schema change (a `student_payment_accounts` table instead of single columns on `students`) and reconciliation logic that checks both, so only build this if/when there's an actual demand for switching back and forth rather than a one-way move.

Webhook receiver added at `api/webhooks/brevo` — same shared-secret-query-param pattern as Sendchamp (`BREVO_WEBHOOK_SECRET`), since Brevo doesn't sign payloads either. **Before/after going live on `main`, confirm both provider dashboards point at the correct deployed webhook URL**: Sendchamp (`api/webhooks/sendchamp?secret=...`), Brevo (`api/webhooks/brevo?secret=...`) — easy for one to get missed or left pointing at a stale/dev URL during a redeploy or domain change. ## 🔐 Auth & onboarding (LAST — deliberately deferred)

Kept until near launch so login friction doesn't slow daily testing.

- [ ] **2FA** — sign-in second factor
- [x] **Logout is broken** — **FIXED 2026-09-14.** `POST /logout` called `supabase.auth.signOut()` then `redirect('/login')`; the `redirect()` threw `NEXT_REDIRECT` before the cleared auth cookies were flushed, so the session cookie survived and the user stayed signed in (root cause of "the same user profile is always logged in / I can't switch users"). Changed POST to `return NextResponse.json({ ok: true })` so the cleared `Set-Cookie` headers reach the browser; the client `UserMenu.handleLogout` already does `window.location.href='/login'` after the fetch, so no route-side redirect is needed. The GET variant (deactivated/scheduled-deletion bounce) already did this correctly with `NextResponse.redirect` and was unchanged. Typecheck clean.
- [ ] **Active sessions / device management** — see & revoke other sessions. Also handle the multi-device / concurrent-login case properly: multiple distinct staff accounts should each stay logged in on their own devices simultaneously (Supabase supports this per-refresh-token by default), and one account signing out on one device shouldn't affect another. Verify once logout is fixed.

## 🔒 Security & go-live hardening

- [x] **Dependabot vulnerabilities cleared (35 → 0)** — **DONE 2026-09-11 (`39f35ad`).** Triaged all 35 open alerts: every one was a third-party dependency CVE, none in our own code, and most didn't reach the running app (the 4 "criticals" were Next.js Windows-RCE + Image-Optimization RCE/DoS — we deploy to Vercel/Linux and use no `next/image`; the SSRF ones need `rewrites`/custom-server, neither of which we have). Fixed by bumping `next` 16.2.9 → **16.3.4** (closed every Next CVE) + `npm audit fix` for the build-time tooling advisories (postcss, browserslist, js-yaml, brace-expansion, baseline-browser-mapping — none serve a runtime request). Verified: `npm audit` → 0, `tsc` clean, production build compiles all 38 routes, dev `/login` 200. Dependabot re-scans the branch async, so its banner clears a little after the push, not instantly. Keep an eye on new alerts as deps update before launch.
- [x] **No login rate limiting** — found during adversarial stress-testing (2026-09-16): the login route accepts unlimited repeated attempts with no lockout, delay, or CAPTCHA, making credential brute-forcing/stuffing feasible against any known email. Needs a rate limit (per-IP and/or per-account) before go-live. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** per-account throttling, not per-IP — this deployment has no Redis/Upstash/KV anywhere (checked), and Vercel's default Node serverless runtime doesn't hold reliable in-memory state across invocations, so the count has to live in Postgres; a new `login_attempts` table (`db/login_attempts.sql`, **needs to be run manually in the Supabase SQL editor**) records every attempt keyed by lower-cased email, written/read only through `createServiceRoleClient()` since this check runs before there's a session to scope RLS by (RLS enabled, zero policies — same pattern already used for the pre-auth webhook tables). `src/lib/auth/loginRateLimit.ts` locks an account out for 15 minutes after 5 failed attempts within a 15-minute window, and `src/app/login/actions.ts`'s `login()` checks the lock before ever calling `signInWithPassword` (so a locked-out guess doesn't even reach Supabase auth) and records the outcome after. A successful login clears that account's failed-attempt history immediately, so earlier typos don't linger against the real owner. Deliberately not per-IP: a school's front desk is usually one shared computer/network behind one IP, and locking that out over one person's mistyped password would be worse than the risk it prevents — throttling the account itself is what actually stops credential stuffing against a known email, which is exactly what this finding demonstrated. No CAPTCHA added — an extra external dependency for a threat the account lockout already closes.
- [ ] **Unauthenticated dev-only routes reachable** — `/simulator`, `api/dev/provision-dva`, `api/dev/simulate-payment` (already tracked below under "Remove dev tooling from production") were confirmed during adversarial testing to be reachable with no auth check in the tested environment, not just present. Removing them before production (existing item below) closes this, but flagging here as a confirmed exploit path, not just leftover dev tooling — treat as a go-live blocker, not a cleanup nice-to-have. **PARTIALLY ADDRESSED 2026-09-16:** the two API routes now require a real staff session + permission (see the CRITICAL finding above) — no longer reachable unauthenticated even on a misconfigured deployment. **`/simulator` deleted outright 2026-09-23** (Phase 09 sweep, line 245 — it was actually git-tracked and live in production with zero auth, a real cross-tenant data leak; confirmed removed from disk 2026-09-25). Remaining close on this item: delete the two dev API routes before go-live (they're permission-gated now, not removed) — 1 of 3 items left, not 3.
- [x] **Missing permission check on a PDF-generation route** — a PDF/document-generation endpoint found during adversarial testing does not verify the requesting user has permission to view the underlying invoice/student before generating the document, allowing a logged-in user from one school context to potentially request a PDF for a record outside their normal access. Needs the same `schoolId`/permission scoping already applied to the equivalent data-read server actions. **FIXED 2026-09-16:** duplicate of the `/api/cycles/[id]/pdf` finding above — see that entry for the fix (both were caught by different stress-test agents against the same route).
- [x] **Self-service password reset (forgot password → email → set new password)** — **DONE 2026-09-16.** There was no way for a staff member who forgot their password to recover it — the login page had no link, and no server action called `resetPasswordForEmail`. Built on the existing PKCE infra that already worked for invite links (`/auth/callback/route.ts`'s `exchangeCodeForSession`, `/set-password/page.tsx`'s post-exchange `updateUser({ password })`) — same "email → code link → callback exchanges it for a session → set-password" flow, just a second entry point into it. New `forgotPassword(formData)` server action (`src/app/login/actions.ts`) calls `resetPasswordForEmail(email, { redirectTo: '<origin>/auth/callback?next=/set-password' })` via an anon Supabase client, and always returns `{ success: true }` regardless of whether the email matches an account (no user-enumeration leak). New `src/app/forgot-password/page.tsx` (email form → "check your inbox" confirmation state), linked from `/login` via a "Forgot password?" link next to the Password field, and added to `middleware.ts`'s `authPaths` so it's reachable while signed out. `/set-password` copy generalized slightly ("Set a new password for your account" / "Checking your link…") since it now serves both first-time invites and password resets. Verified the dispatch side directly against Supabase's real `/auth/v1/recover` REST endpoint (no browser needed for that half); the full email → callback → set-password round trip still needs a live browser test by the user.
- [ ] **Migrate `middleware.ts` → `proxy.ts` (Next 16.3 deprecation)** — surfaced by the 16.3.4 bump: Next now prints a `middleware-to-proxy` deprecation notice on dev/build. Middleware still runs correctly in 16.3.x (verified — auth gating works, `/login` 200), so this is not urgent, but the rename becomes mandatory in a future major. Next ships a codemod: `npx @next/codemod@canary middleware-to-proxy .`. **Do before go-live** so the migration is verified calmly rather than forced by a later forced upgrade. Deliberately kept out of the security-patch commit to avoid muddying it. Only `src/middleware.ts` is affected (its matcher config + the exported function); re-verify auth redirects and the route matcher after running the codemod.
- [ ] **Credential rotation** — rotate all keys before production
- [ ] **Sandbox → production swaps** — Monnify (+ any other) sandbox → live
- [ ] **GitHub Actions as a two-factor backup for every daily Vercel cron** — Vercel's crons (`vercel.json`: reconcile, reminders, message-sweep, purge-deletions, job-sweep) are the only thing running that work; if Vercel's cron silently misses a run, there's no second trigger for up to 24h. Same free-GitHub-Actions approach already built for `job-sweep` (`.github/workflows/job-sweep.yml`, needs `APP_URL` + `SWEEP_SECRET` repo secrets, currently un-activatable — no stable prod domain or secret yet) — extend it (or add one workflow per route) to hit each of the other 4 cron endpoints too, so a missed Vercel cron gets caught by GitHub within minutes instead of waiting for the next day's run. **User confirmed: set this up at go-live**, once there's a real production domain to point the secrets at.
- [x] **Run `db/rls_privilege_fixes.sql` against production Supabase** — self-escalation/deactivation lockdown. **Owner confirmed run 2026-09-25.**
- [ ] **Remove dev tooling from production** — `api/dev/provision-dva`, `api/dev/simulate-payment` (`/simulator` already deleted 2026-09-23, see line 245/433)
- [ ] **DB test-data cleanup** — clear seeded test school/students (folds into the fresh DB rebuild above)
- [x] **Audit log UI** — built & browser-tested (2026-08-28). `/settings/audit-log` shows a colour-coded, module-pill table (When · Who · Action · Details · Module) covering ~55 privileged staff actions (students, families, classes, sessions, terms, invoices, fee structure, discounts, settings, account security) via `logAuditEvent` call sites across every action file. Scoped to real staff actions only — automated events (webhook payments, reconciliation runs) were deliberately kept out and continue to surface only in Recent Activity. Gated on its own `see-audit-log` permission (separate from `manage-settings`, superseding the note in the "Settings pages self-check" entry above). CSV export lives on the Reports page (`type=audit-log`), with human-readable action labels and no raw target IDs/metadata. Files: `src/components/settings/AuditLogTable.tsx`, `src/lib/audit/{logAudit,auditLog,auditLogGroups,auditLogLabels}.ts`.
- [x] **Data & privacy** (`/settings/data-privacy`, owner-only) — **Phase 1 built & tested:** transparency sections (live "what we store" inventory counts, sub-processor list, security summary, retention statement), one-click **"download all my data"** as a dependency-free `.zip` of per-table CSVs (`src/lib/dataPrivacy/`, `src/lib/reports/zip.ts`, export route at `settings/data-privacy/export`), plus contact + policy links (`fees101.com/privacy` + `/terms`, confirmed live). **Phase 2 built & TESTED end-to-end (2026-08-27, pushed):** scheduled account-deletion flow. Owner opens the "Close account & delete data" dialog (export-first prompt + required acknowledge checkbox + type-school-name confirm) → `requestAccountDeletion` writes a `school_deletion_requests` row (status `scheduled`, `scheduled_for` = now+30d, `financial_purge_at` = now+6y), deactivates every staff login immediately, logs `account.deletion_scheduled`, and signs the owner out. Login/kick-out now distinguishes a self-closed account (dated "scheduled for deletion — contact support" message, `?error=scheduled_deletion`) from an admin deactivation. Daily cron `/api/admin/purge-deletions` (CRON_SECRET / `PURGE_SECRET`, 03:15) runs `archive_and_delete_school()` past the grace window (archives anonymised financials — no names/free-text — into `archived_financials`, then FK-ordered hard-delete of all school-scoped rows + the school, then deletes auth users), and `purge_expired_financials()` drops archived rows past 6y. Reminders cron skips schools with a scheduled deletion. Purge executor verified against a throwaway school via `scripts/test-purge.mjs` (all rows gone + auth user 404s + 2 anonymised rows archived + request→completed). **Go-live setup:** re-run `db/data_privacy.sql` in the prod Supabase project; generate `CRON_SECRET` (required) + `PURGE_SECRET` (optional) in Vercel env. Files: `db/data_privacy.sql`, `src/lib/dataPrivacy/{deletion,config}.ts`, `settings/data-privacy/actions.ts`, `components/settings/DeleteAccountSection.tsx`, `api/admin/purge-deletions/route.ts`. Restore/cancel during grace is admin-side (line 140). **Export pagination + audit-log bug fixed and live-verified 2026-09-24:** the "download all my data" export used an unranged `.select('*')` per table, silently truncating any table past PostgREST's 1000-row cap, and never included `audit_log` at all despite the UI copy promising it. `buildFullExport` (`src/lib/dataPrivacy/exportAll.ts`) now pages every table via `.range()` until a short/empty page, and `audit_log` was added to `EXPORT_TABLES`. Verified live end-to-end (not just `tsc`-clean): triggered a real export as the authenticated owner, downloaded and unzipped the result — all 15 expected CSVs plus README present with correct row counts, including `audit-log.csv` (320 real rows, previously zero).
- [x] **Whole-product permission coverage audit (post-redesign) — 2026-09-22.** Owner asked for a full pass across every settings/editable surface, not just the redesign's own recent changes, to make sure the visual rebuild didn't drop or miss a permission check anywhere. Four parallel audits covered every `actions.ts` under Students, Invoices, Fees (structure/cycles/year-end), Payments, Discounts, and all of Settings (school profile, academic structure, account security, data & privacy, discount policy, payment config, reminders, roles & users) against `permissionCatalog.ts`, cross-checked server `requirePermission`/`can` calls against client `useCan` gates. **Found and fixed:** (1) `regenerateCancelledInvoice` (`students/[id]/actions.ts`) checked `manage-students` server-side while its only caller gated the button on `manage-invoices` — a manage-students-only user could've called it directly and bypassed the intended invoice permission; now requires `manage-invoices`, matching the UI and the catalog's own definition of that key. (2) Three cosmetic client-side gaps where the server already enforced correctly but no matching `useCan` hid the control, so a user without the right permission saw a dead button that would fail on click: Academic Structure's "Current term" MANAGE action (now hidden without `manage-fee-structure`, matching its "Current session" sibling row), the "Mark as resolved" unresolved-credit button on the student fees tab (now behind `manage-students`), and the "Cancel invoice"/"Withdraw and cancel it" options in the student withdraw/graduate flow (now behind `manage-invoices`). Everything else — Roles & Users' self-escalation/cap-delegation/owner-immutability rules, Data & Privacy's hardcoded owner-only gates, the discount auto-approval threshold's loophole-resistance, the school-email-verification token's security — checked out clean. **Not a bug, just a note for future redesign passes:** several fee/cycle/year-end panels (`CreateTermPanel`, `EditFeeGroupPanel`, `FeeFormPanel`, `GenerateInvoicesPanel`, `ManageOptInsPanel`, `CloseTermLedger`, `YearEndRolloverWizard`) carry no permission check of their own — safe today only because every current entry point into them is already gated by a parent component or page. If a future redesign adds a second, more direct entry point to any of these, it would silently inherit no protection — worth gating these panels directly rather than relying on their callers, next time one of them is touched.
- [~] **School Profile contact-detail verification (2026-09-22) — built, migrations pending.** Owner reasoning: the school barely gives its email/phone to parents, but Fees101 itself may need to reach the school, so both should be at least confirmed live rather than typo'd-and-forgotten. **Email — built earlier this session, migration run by owner:** a changed email resets verification and gets a fresh confirm-link (`crypto.randomBytes(32)` token, `/api/verify-school-email` route on the service-role client since there's no session pre-click), row shows "Verified" (green) or "Verification pending" (ochre) + Resend. `db/school_email_verification.sql` — **owner confirmed run.** **Phone — built now, same session, migration NOT yet run:** since a phone can't be "clicked," this is a 6-digit SMS OTP instead — `sendSchoolPhoneVerification` texts a code via the existing Sendchamp `sendMessage()` (new `phone_verify` message type), stores only its SHA-256 hash + a 10-minute expiry + an attempt counter (never the code itself), and `verifySchoolPhoneOtp` checks it (5 wrong tries max, then a fresh code is required). Changing the phone number auto-sends a code and opens the entry drawer immediately in the same save; the row's "Verify" button (when pending) reuses a still-live code instead of re-sending, and the entry drawer has its own cooldown-gated "Resend code" link (60s). New actions in `settings/actions.ts`: `startSchoolPhoneVerification`, `resendSchoolPhoneVerification`, `verifySchoolPhoneOtp`, all gated on `manage-school-profile` like every other School Profile action. Audit-logged as `school.phone_verified`. `db/school_phone_verification.sql` (adds `phone_verified_at`/`phone_verify_code_hash`/`phone_verify_expires_at`/`phone_verify_attempts`/`phone_verify_sent_at` to `schools`, and adds `phone_verify` to `message_logs`'s allowed message types) — **owner confirmed run 2026-09-25.** tsc clean; not yet browser-tested.

## 🌱 Future / bigger bets (post-V1)

- [ ] **In-product engagement: daily hints + setup guide bot** — **depends on the in-app notification system being built first** (no general notification center exists yet — `admin_notifications` today is a narrow staff-alert table, not a user-facing feed/bell). Two connected ideas, captured here for later design:
  - **"Did you know" daily tips** — a short, dismissible card/toast that shows one random tip about the product each time a user logs in (e.g. "Did you know you can print all of a class's invoices at once to include in report cards?", "Did you know you can send a reminder to just the students who haven't paid?"). Pick randomly from a growing tip pool each session — repeats are fine, the point is passive discovery for features people aren't using, not a guided sequence. Read/dismissed state should persist per-user so a dismissed tip doesn't reappear immediately, but the pool is small enough early on that repeats will happen naturally. Needs: a `product_tips` table or static content list, a per-user "last seen tip" or "dismissed tip ids" record, and the notification/toast surface to render it in once that exists.
  - **Setup guide mascot for new schools** — an animated character (needs its own visual design — a small mascot/bot with personality, not a generic tooltip) that greets a brand-new school's first login and walks them through how the account is set up (academic structure → fee structure → students → first invoice, roughly). Same character doubles as the one "speaking" the daily tips above once onboarding is done, so it becomes a recurring, recognizable presence rather than a one-time wizard. Needs: an onboarding-checklist/progress state per school (so it knows what's already done and doesn't repeat), the mascot's animation/illustration, and a decision on whether it's a fixed step-by-step tour or a "click me for the next thing" companion the user can dismiss and recall.
  - Sequencing: build the notification system first (the delivery mechanism), then the tips content/dismissal state, then the mascot on top — the mascot is presentation/personality on top of the same delivery mechanism, not a separate system.
- [ ] **Parent-facing portal** — parent login to view invoices, balances, pay
- [ ] **Per-student one-off charges** — fines, replacement books, individual exam/late fees as ad-hoc invoice lines. *Deferred: build only if a school asks. Workaround for now = add an optional fee (e.g. "Damages") and leave a note.*
- [ ] **Installment / part-payment plans** — structured "pay in N" schedules (partial payment already works; formal plans deferred until a school needs them)
- [ ] **Automatic late fees** — penalty added when an invoice goes overdue (deferred until requested)
- [ ] **Standalone Payments operations page** — feed, unapplied credits, manual entry, "reconcile now" (overlaps with reporting/analytics above)
- [ ] **Collection-by-class operations page** — drill-down stats (separate from Settings academic structure)
- [ ] **Per-school custom email templates** — Brevo (unlike SES) supports designer/saved templates per sender, so a school could eventually customize wording/branding of their own invoice/receipt/reminder emails beyond just the logo. Deferred until a school actually asks for it.
- [ ] **Per-message channel selection UI** — a "Messages" tab where staff pick a message type (invoice/receipt/reminder) for a student and explicitly choose SMS, email, or both, instead of relying on the default policy. Useful if a parent complains they're not receiving one channel. The channel-override plumbing (`channelOverride` on `sendInvoice`, `sendManualReminder`) already exists per-invoice; this would surface it as its own dedicated page/tab rather than a one-off resend button.
- [ ] **Make the dashboard's Quick Actions panel real** — the mockup's "Record payment / Generate invoice / Send reminder" tiles should each be a genuine standalone action from the dashboard itself (student/invoice picker → the actual flow), not just links out to another page. Needs its own design pass — each action already exists elsewhere in the app (student page, cycles page); the work here is building a well-thought-out consolidated entry point, not new underlying functionality. v2/v3-scale polish, not urgent.
- [ ] **App-wide term/session time-travel view** — a global "viewing: this term / last term / a future draft term" switcher (distinct from the scope selector already on the analytics/payments page, which is comparison-focused rather than "browse the whole app as of a past period") that would let the dashboard — and ideally other pages — show historical data as it stood in an older cycle, the same way you use the app today but looking backward. Explicitly a hard, open-ended one: reasoning through what "the whole app in the past" even means per page (which data is period-scoped vs always-current) is a real design problem, not a quick toggle. Not slotted into any particular version — pick up only when there's nothing higher-priority to work on.
- [ ] **Group classes into arms/streams (e.g. Primary 1A/1B/1C under "Primary 1")** — today each arm is already its own independent `classes` row (confirmed 2026-09-03); there's no schema relationship tying Primary 1A/1B/1C together as "the same grade level," just a shared name prefix by convention. For a big school this matters for organizing/filtering, not fees: each arm has a different class teacher and a different set of students, and bulk operations (e.g. printing all of Primary 1's invoices) currently mean re-sorting students by arm by hand every time instead of the arms already being grouped for you. **Recommended approach:** add a thin *optional* grouping layer on top, not a rework of the class model — a nullable `classes.grade_level_id` (or similar) referencing a new lightweight group table (school-scoped, school names it however they like, e.g. "Primary 1"). Fee structure, promotion mapping, and invoicing all keep working exactly as they do today, unaffected — each arm still has its own fee assignments and its own "promotes to" target, since arms can differ there even if usually mirrored. The group only feeds filtering/sorting/bulk-selection (student list, reports, bulk invoice printing) so a school can act on "the whole grade level" as one unit without losing the per-arm data underneath. Class-teacher assignment is a related but separate gap — no teacher concept exists anywhere in the schema today — track separately, don't bundle into this. **Deferred:** meaningful scope (new table + group-management UI + wiring into student list/reports/bulk-invoice filters) for a need that only bites at bigger-school scale; revisit as a post-V1 "big schools" feature rather than building now.
- [ ] **Per-fee-item payment allocation (pay for "just tuition," track "club" as separately unpaid)** — confirmed 2026-09-03: payments today apply against a student's outstanding invoices as one lump sum, oldest term first (`applyProviderPayment`/`applyPayment.ts`), with no concept of which specific fee line within an invoice got covered. A parent can't pay only tuition and leave club fees outstanding in a way the app understands — it's all just "invoice outstanding amount," so there's no way to know the student is fine for core school access but excluded from a specific paid extra (club, trip, etc.) until that's settled too. Real feature, not a small tweak — would need payments to allocate against specific `fee_items`/invoice line rows rather than the invoice total, a per-item paid/unpaid status, and some UI for a parent (or staff) to choose what a payment is *for* rather than just "pay outstanding balance." Naturally pairs with future WhatsApp/messaging prompts ("pay for club" as its own explicit action/prompt rather than a lump reminder). No design work done yet beyond naming the gap — revisit once there's a settled flow to build it against, not now.
